NOTICE OF PUBLIC HEARING AND AGENDA
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS
Tuesday, September 26, 2023
ONE OR MORE SUPERVISORS MAY ATTEND VIA VIDEO AND/OR TELEPHONICALLY 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors will hold a meeting open to the public on Tuesday, September 26, 2023, in the
Supervisors’ Chamber in the Navajo County Governmental Complex, 100 East Code Talkers Drive, Holbrook, Arizona. The
Board may vote to meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any item as indicated
on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A) (3). 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors finds that it is in the best interest of the community to conduct this public meeting using
a hybrid method in order to serve the public interest. 
Join Zoom Meeting, click here: https://us06web.zoom.us/j/87692173340?pwd=WDU3WWhlZFcxd1JqN1BzYXI1dUdpZz09 
 
Call In Number: 1-253-215-8782 OR 1-346-248-7799 (You may use either teleconference number)
Meeting ID: 876 9217 3340
Passcode: 271616
Persons with disabilities who need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s
office at (928) 524-4053; at least 48 hours prior to the meeting, so an accommodation can be arranged.
Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at the Chairman’s discretion. A copy of the agenda background material
provided to the Board, except items to be considered in executive session, is available for public inspection at Navajo County
website, www.navajocountyaz.gov
“NOTICE TO PARENTS AND LEGAL GUARDIANS: Parents and legal guardians have the right to consent before Navajo County makes a video
or voice recording of a minor child, pursuant to A.R.S. § 1-602(A)(9). The Navajo County Board of Supervisors regular meetings are recorded and
may be viewed on Navajo County’s website, and social media pages. If you permit your child to attend/participate in a televised Navajo County
Board of Supervisors meeting, a recording will be made, and your child’s picture may be posted on Navajo County’s social media pages. You may
exercise your right not to consent by not allowing your child to attend/participate in the meeting.” 
9:00 a.m. Call To Order: Invocation and Pledge of Allegiance
           
1. CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Individuals may address the Board on any relevant issue for up to
3 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Board members may not respond to any
comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be
placed on a future agenda.
 
 
2. CONSENT AGENDA: These items are considered to be routine or administrative in nature
and will be approved in a single motion. A Board member may request for any item to be
removed from the consent agenda and considered on the regular agenda:
 
 
a. Minutes: September 12, 2023  
 
b. Clerk of Superior Court Report, August 2023  
 
c. Constable Reports: Snowflake Precinct #3, Show Low Precinct #5, Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct
#6, August 2023

<!-- page 2 -->
d. Justice Court Reports: Holbrook Precinct #1,  Winslow Precinct #2, Snowflake Precinct #3,
Kayenta Precinct #4, Show Low Precinct #5, Pinetop-Lakeside Precinct #6, August 2023
 
 
e. Certification of Fiscal Year 2023 Fill the Gap Revenue Pursuant to A.R.S. §41-2421G  
 
f. Reissuance of Stale Dated Checks  
 
g. Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution: 
Community Development Block Grants (CDBG) Technical Assistance Contract
 
 
h. Amendment No. 5 of the Annual Contract for Vehicle Replacement Program No. B19-08-019
with Show Low Ford to include additional pricing for the remaining 2024 models that were not
included in Amendment  No. 4.
 
 
i. Sympathy Letters: Anne Frank (Health District)  
 
3. EMPLOYEE RECOGNITION:  
 
a. Navajo County recognizes the following employees for their excellence in personal
performance, far exceeding organization expectations for the month of September: Crystal
Castillo (BOS/Administration) and Kathryn Mathewson (Health District)
 
 
Presented By: Lea Petersen
 
4. ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
 
a. Presentation to Chris Frayer on her retirement after 23+ years with Navajo County.  
Presented By: Eric Scott/Cammy Darris
 
b. Presentation on Programs offered by Navajo County Cooperative Extension  
Presented By: Mike Hauser, Director/Lori Jaramillo, Business Manager
 
c. Presentation on Navajo County's grant development and management update.  
Presented By: Ken Maruyama

<!-- page 3 -->
5. NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors
Session: 
 
 
a. REGULAR AGENDA:  
 
1. Consideration of amendment to the contract between Navajo County and Care 1st Health Plan
of Arizona to reflect updated pricing effective October 1, 2023.    The new pricing is set forth
in the new "Schedule A."
 
Presented By: Jason Moore
 
2. Consideration of Navajo County Proclamation for Suicide Prevention Awareness Month 2023.  
Presented By: Janelle Linn and Ciera Hensley
 
6. ELECTIONS:  
 
a. Presentation on the Secretary of State E-Qual Candidate Online Petition  
Presented By: Rayleen Richards
 
b. Consideration of adoption of Resolution Number 34-2023, adopting the new voting precincts
maps that were adjusted to meet the New Board of Supervisor Districts pursuant to A.R.S.
16-411.
 
Presented By: Rayleen Richards/Jason Moore/Ryan Taylor
 
7. PUBLIC WORKS:  
 
a. Consideration of approving the renewal of an Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) with the
Navajo Nation and Navajo County. This IGA has an attached task agreement (Attachment A)
to fund two equipment operators in the amount of $120,000.00 per year for road maintenance
projects for public roads within Navajo County. 
 
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
 
b. Consideration of approval to enter into an Intergovernmental Agreement between the State of
Arizona and Navajo County for the Bourdon Ranch Road & Roundup Drive Left Turn Lane
Project.
 
Presented By: Madhav Mundle

<!-- page 4 -->
c. PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT:  
 
1. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of the adoption of Resolution
35-2023 approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 80’
monopole wireless communication tower and associated facilities on a vacant, 13.15-acre
parcel, in the White Mountain Lakes area.
 
Presented By: Cody Cooper
 
2. PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution 36-2023,
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 100' mono-elm
wireless communication tower and associated facilities in the Linden
Area. APN# 409-22-005C.
 
Presented By: Tyler Richards
 
d. NAVAJO COUNTY FLOOD CONTROL DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  
 
1. CONSENT AGENDA: The following item(s) are considered to be of a routine or
administrative in nature and will be approved as a group unless a member of the Board
wishes to discuss a particular item: 
 
 
a. Navajo County's Community Rating System Annual Recertification Letter  
 
8. COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney and
Board members.
 
 
 
9. ADJOURN  
 
Dated: September 22, 2023 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
Posted: 2:00 p.m.  By: lt                         
Leah Thomas - Deputy Clerk of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. e.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Certification of Fiscal Year 2023 Fill the Gap Revenue Pursuant to A.R.S.
§41-2421G
Submitted By: Lu Ann Garbini, Court Administrator 
Department: Superior Court
Motion before the Board:
Certification of Fiscal Year 2023 Fill the Gap Revenue Pursuant to A.R.S. §41-2421G
Background:
Pursuant to A.R.S. §41-2421G, "each county board of supervisors shall certify if the total
revenues received by the justice courts and the superior court, including the clerk of the superior
court, exceed the amount received in fiscal year 1997-1998".
The revenue detail report is submitted as supporting documentation. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY2024 
Budgeted Y/N: N
Amount Requested: 0
Fiscal Impact:
No fiscal impact. Compliance to statutory requirement to certify revenues. 
Attachments

Revenue Detail Report

CERTIFICATION STATEMENT 
The Navajo County Board of Supervisors hereby certifies that, pursuant to A.R.S. § 41-2421 C: 
1. Navajo County collects certain monies from the Superior Court, including the Clerk of 
the Court, Justice Courts Precincts,# 1, #2, #3, #4, #5, and #6 in Navajo County for the 
payment of filing fees, including clerk fees, diversion fees, adult and juvenile probation 
fees, juvenile monetary assessments, fines, penalties, surcharges, sanctions and 
forfeitures (with the exception of the Clean Elections Fund 10% surcharge, restitution, 
child support and bonds); and  
 
2. Navajo County collections for base year of 1997-1998 were $2,061,993.97; and 
 
3. Navajo County collections for fiscal year 2022-2023 were 5% in excess of the base year 
collections; and  
 
4. Five percent (5%) of all collections during fiscal year 2022-2023 were "set aside" to 
comply with the Fill the Gap legislation in A.RS. 41-2421 C; and  
 
5. The total of the collections during fiscal Year 2022-2023 allocable to the 5% "set aside" 
Fill the Gap fund is: $206,715.40. 
 
 
 
  
NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
 _____________________________________ 
       Alberto L. Peshlakai, Chairman 
 
 
ATTEST

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. f.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Reissuance of Stale Dated Checks
Submitted For: Kari Lopez, Treasurer  Submitted By: Olivia Todd, Chief Deputy
Treasurer
Department: Treasurer
Motion before the Board:
Reissuance of Stale Dated Checks
Background:
Treasurer's checks were issued as refunds for overpaid property taxes or for investor payments.
The checks were voided because they had been outstanding for a year or more. The payees
were contacted and are requesting replacement checks. All have completed the required
Affidavit For Issuance of Duplicate Warrant form. Upon approval, a new check will be issued to
the individual(s) pursuant to A.R.S. §11-632.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. g.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Community Development Block Grants (CDBG) Technical Assistance Contract
between Northern Arizona Council of Governments (NACOG) and Navajo County
Submitted By: Kenichi Maruyama, Strategic Initiatives Manager
Department: Administrative Services Division: Finance
Motion before the Board:
Contracts signed by County Manager pursuant to Board of Supervisors Resolution:  Community
Development Block Grants (CDBG) Technical Assistance Contract
Background:
Acknowledgment of a fully executed contract ($34,000) for NACOG to provide technical
assistance service for the County's CDBG administration and project management and to ensure
compliance with federal regulations.  Navajo County receives CDBG funds ($512,140) from the
federal government. NACOG CDBG staff assist the County in administering the funds in
compliance with federal regulations. NACOG estimates $34,000 for the County's CDBG
technical assistance and project management service. The County may request a reimbursement
of that expense from the CDBG funds. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2024
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
New fund 291975 has been set up. The County will make payments to NACOG. Then, the
County will get reimbursed from its CDBG funds.

Board of Supervisors Regular 2. h.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Amendment No. 5, B19-08-019 Annual Contract for Vehicle Replacement Program
Submitted For: Bryan Layton, County Manager 
Submitted By: Cheryl Hollins, Account Specialist
Department: Administrative Services
Motion before the Board:
Amendment No. 5 of the Annual Contract for Vehicle Replacement Program No. B19-08-019
with Show Low Ford to include additional pricing for the remaining 2024 models that were not
included in Amendment  No. 4.
Background:
On September 12, 2023, the Board approved Amendment No. 4 to the Annual Contract for the
Vehicle Replacement Program with Show Low Ford, extending Contract No. B19-08-019 for
one additional year and updating the pricing for the 2024 model year and specifications that
were available at the time.  Ford has now released pricing for Group 3 Vans and Group 4 Pick
Up Trucks, which are included under Amendment No. 5. Staff recommends approval of
Amendment No. 5.

LIVESTOCK AND RANGE 
AGRICULTURE AND NATURAL RESOURCE 
 
 
 
 
 
 
QR Code for 
Northeastern AZ 
Agriculture and 
Livestock Face-
Highlights for 2022:  
 
Livestock:  
 100 attendees at the Northern Arizona Livestock Producers Work-
shop in Holbrook, that included Apache County producers 
 70 attendees at the Producers and Agency Meeting/Mexican Wolf 
Outreach Day 
 40 attendees of the Virtual Livestock Wellness Series  
Range:  
 Over 4 miles of fence repair or replacement completed on the Bear 
Fire Fence Grant  
 Evaluated over 60 acres of different rangelands for toxic plants and 
range conditions  
 Supported several ranchers during ALIRT calls during and after the 
situation  
General Agriculture:  
 150 attendees (virtual and in-person) at the Beginning Farmer 
Rancher Workshop in Snowflake, including many from 
Apache County. 
 Grew 100 pounds of produce for seed that will be used to 
stock local seed libraries at the Holbrook and St. Johns 
Demonstration Garden  
Apache County Livestock and Range Cooperative Extension Impacts: 
• Education on livestock care, treatment and necessities to new and 
current ranchers and farmers helped bring a new awareness 
around appropriate livestock care. 
• Pasture Management outreach has helped landowners identify the 
correct forage for their specific livestock.

<!-- page 35 -->
4-H 
YOUTH DEVELOPMENT 
  
 
BR 4-H Fab Lab Update 
-Open House and Expo was September 12 to solicit problems/issues 
from community members or organizations within our community that 
can be solved by the students using STEAM.  
-The Hydroponics System designed by the students is a low-cost 
modular system capable of being assembled to create either an indoor 
hydroponics system, a urban balcony system or even a green wall de-
sign over 10 feet tall.    
-The 4-H Watershed Preservation Team is developing a system that 
will be capable of conducting all water quality testing necessary to 
complete the non-source point pollution tracking required to identify 
sources of contamination within our surface and groundwater.   
-The 4-H fab lab students worked with Dr Greene, University of Arizona 
Cooperative Equine Specialist, to 3D print an Equine spine for use in 
state-wide equine educational programs. 
The Navajo County 4-H Small Stock program host-
ed the first-ever state-wide 4-H Small Stock Camp 
and Show in Taylor on June 8 – 10, 2023. More 
than 75 youth attended the three day event.  
Youth evaluations showed that it was rated 9.1 out 
of 10 (1 being Poor and 10 being Excellent) and par-
ents rated it 9.6 out of 10.  Every participant stated 
that they would attend the camp next year. 
 Navajo County 4-H Youth Development Numbers: 
A review of the Navajo County 4-H Youth Development Program numbers over the past two years pro-
vides a snapshot of the program.  We are working to keep the 
trend moving upward. 
2022     2023 
206 youth enrolled   213 youth enrolled 
50 new youth enrolled   65 new youth enrolled 
Swine—85 youth   Swine—91 youth

<!-- page 36 -->
FCHS 
FAMILY HEALTH / CONSUMER SCIENCE 
 
      
Navajo County FCHS Impacts: 
Because of the University of Navajo County Cooperative Extension SNAP program we completed the 5th annual 
Park Summer School Garden Program. Twenty-two students completed the program, the pre-test average was a 
16.96%, the post-test average was 96.3%. Students also reported that they either had or wanted to start a gar-
den at home, and that the class was helpful in knowing how to take care of the garden.  
Because of the food preservation workshops that were offered in Holbrook participants stated that they would 
use or purchase a traditional dehydrator and that they would make use of the freeze dehydrator at the office. 
We have already had community residents reserve the freeze dehydrator for personal use. 
 
       Public Outreach focused on 
supporting residents in Nava-
jo County by providing train-
ing, support, and resources 
to support food systems 
(gardens, food preservation), 
active living (physical activity 
events), and school health 
(gardens, local wellness poli-
cies, wellness committees, 
staff training).

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Discussion and Action on Contract Amendment with Care 1st for Mental Health
Screening and Evaluations
Submitted For: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney 
Submitted By: Jason Moore, Deputy County Attorney
Department: County Attorney
Presented By: Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of amendment to the contract between Navajo County and Care 1st Health Plan
of Arizona to reflect updated pricing effective October 1, 2023.    The new pricing is set forth in
the new "Schedule A."
Background:
Pricing changes are to reflect current market pricing and is typical of fees being paid by other
counties for similar screening and evaluation services.
Recommendation: 
Recommend approval.
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: 2023-2024
Budgeted Y/N:
Amount Requested:
Fiscal Impact:
The reflected pricing means that Navajo County will need to budget approximately $100,000
more annually for mental health screening and evaluation expenses.

Board of Supervisors Regular 5. a. 2.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Navajo County Proclamation Suicide Prevention Awareness Month 2023
Submitted For: Janelle Linn, Health Director 
Submitted By: Ciera Hensley, Mental Health Program Manager
Department: NC Public Health Services District
Presented By: Janelle Linn and Ciera Hensley
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of Navajo County Proclamation for Suicide Prevention Awareness Month 2023.
Background:
The month of September 2023 is Suicide Prevention Awareness Month. Suicide has had a
profound and lasting impact on families, friends, businesses, schools, and communities
throughout Navajo County, as it is the 10th leading cause of death. Also, in 2020, suicide was
the second leading cause of death in Arizona for adolescents aged 15-19. This proclamation has
the potential to spread life-saving information such as the availability of the 988 Suicide and
Crisis Lifeline, remind Navajo County residents of their role in prevention efforts, and
recognize those who are working endlessly to prevent suicide. Proclaiming Suicide Prevention
Awareness Month in September 2023 in Navajo County can help create a community where
every person has opportunity to be treated free from stigma, to promotes understanding of
mental health, and work to implement initiatives to move toward ending preventable deaths by
suicide. 
Recommendation: 
Recommend that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors proclaim September 2023 as Suicide
Prevention Awareness Month in Navajo County.
Attachments
NC Suicide Prevention Awareness Month 
Form Review
Inbox Reviewed By Date
Health Director Janelle Linn 09/19/2023 09:34 AM
Health Director Janelle Linn 09/19/2023 09:34 AM

<!-- page 57 -->
Navajo County               
Proclamation 
 
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
SUICIDE PREVENTION AWARENESS MONTH 2023 
WHEREAS, the month of September 2023 is Suicide Prevention Awareness Month.  Suicide has had a 
profound and lasting impact on the families, friends, businesses, schools and communities throughout 
Navajo County; and   
WHEREAS, in Navajo County, suicide is the 10th leading cause of death; and 
WHEREAS, in 2020 suicide was the second leading cause of death in Arizona for adolescents aged 15-19; 
and 
WHEREAS, no less than 39 Navajo County residents were lost to suicide in 2022; and  
WHEREAS, approximately one in five adults and adolescents will experience a mental health challenge at 
some point in their lives, and   
WHEREAS, anyone that may be experiencing a mental health-related distress or are worried about a 
loved one who may need crisis support can connect 24/7 with a trained crisis counselor for free at the 
988 Suicide and Crisis Lifeline by Call or text 988 or Chat at www.988lifeline.org; and  
WHEREAS, every Navajo County resident has a role in preventing suicide through education, awareness 
and stigma reduction. More tools, information and resources can be found at www.988lifeline.org; and  
WHEREAS, The Board of Supervisors would like to officially recognize all of those who have lost a loved one 
to suicide, have attempted suicide or have suicidal ideations, and all of the behavioral health workers, 
educators, health providers, parents, and friends who are working endlessly to prevent suicide.  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT PROCLAIMED, by the Board of Supervisors of Navajo County that 
September of 2023 is designated as “Suicide Prevention Awareness Month” in Navajo County and would like 
to recognize the role of the county in supporting a community where every person is treated free from stigma, 
promotes understanding of mental health, and works to put an end to preventable deaths such as suicide. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED by the Board of Supervisors on SEPTEMBER 26, 2023. 
         ____________________________ 
Alberto Peshlakai, Chairman

<!-- page 69 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
New Precinct 
Boundaries 2023

<!-- page 70 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
What are Voting Precincts 
• The basic geographical areas for organizing and administering 
elections
• A.R.S 16-411 (A) – The Board of Supervisors of each county, on or 
before October 1 of each year preceding the year of the general 
election, by an order, shall establish a convenient number of elections 
precincts in the county and define the boundaries of the precincts.
• Election precincts are drawn “so as to be included within election 
districts for elected officers of the state and its political subdivisions.”  
A.R.S. 16-411(A)(1).

<!-- page 71 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Drawing Precincts From the 
Ground Up 
Precinct Foundations:
• Congressional and Legislative District boundaries (cannot cross precinct 
lines)
• Board of Supervisors Districts/Community College Precincts 
• Justice Court Precincts
• School District Boundaries
Special Taxing Districts:
• Fire Districts
• Water Districts
• Flood Control Districts
• Sanitary Districts
• Hospital Districts
• Recreational Districts

<!-- page 72 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Examples of Ways Precincts are 
Used 
• Laying out ballot
• Used to rotate candidates' names on ballots
• Reporting election results
• Election of Political Party Precinct Committemen

<!-- page 73 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Existing  Voting 
Precincts:
14 Precincts
Proposed Voting 
Precincts: 
14 Precincts
No change in the number of precincts is 
being proposed.  Modifications are made to 
reflect new supervisorial District lines that 
became effective in 2022.

<!-- page 74 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
No change 
to polling 
locations - 
39 Vote 
Centers

<!-- page 75 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Suggestions received by BOS for drawing precincts:
 (1) Comply with A.R.S. 16-411(A)(1)
 (2) Take into account tribal election boundaries.
 (3) Take into account census designated places (CDP’s)
 (4) Let’s have more precincts
• We already comply with A.R.S. 16-411(A)(1).  All of our existing precincts are already included within 
election district boundaries for the state and its political subdivisions.  It is practically impossible to draw 
precinct boundaries in a way that they will not contain overlapping election district boundaries.  See 
diagram later in presentation.
• We do not conduct elections or tribal offices.  Navajo County’s precincts have nothing to do with tribal 
elections.  In any event, Tribal Lands cross county lines and some cross State lines. We cannot precinct 
outside the county or outside of the State.
• CDP’s are constructs created by the federal government that assist us in redistricting.  They are not 
elections districts.
• THERE IS NO STATUTORY LIMIT ON THE SIZE OF PRECINCTS.   Size does not matter.
• More precincts = More Problems 
• Many more ballot styles would be necessary
• Many ballot styles confuse poll workers and can lead to a voter receiving the wrong ballot style.  If the voter receives the 
wrong voter style, he/she may vote in a race he/she should not be voting in, or be denied the opportunity to vote in a race 
he/she should be voting in.
• More precincts increase the chance of error.
• Trouble finding sufficient numbers of precinct committeemen

<!-- page 76 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Does the size of a precinct impact a person’s ability to vote?
• No, Precinct lines have become irrelevant to voting when we began using the Vote Center model.  
The model we use allows any voter to appear at any Precinct at any Vote Center in Navajo County 
and they will still be able to vote their correct ballot.
• The Vote Center model is a feature that allows more voter participation rather than an impediment.  
It avoids voters driving around in frustration because he/she cannot find the correct polling place.
• The Vote Center model has completely eliminated the use of provisional ballots in Navajo County 
caused by voters casting ballots in the wrong precinct.
Does the size of a precinct impact the county’s ability to efficiently and accurately tabulate 
votes?
• No.   Fewer precincts do, however, make tabulations reports easier to read.

Navajo County - For Official Use Only
Oct. 13, 201 
Board Of 
Supervisors 
Meeting 
approved Super 
Precincts and 
Vote Centers 
Model 
• Then Recorder Laura Sanchez, 
brought to the board the plan 
to go to this new Super 
Precincts
• It reduced the precincts from 61 to 
14
• Did not reduce the Polling 
Locations, they have remained the 
same to this day. 39 Locations
• It has cut down on ballots, and 
ballot styles
• Less confusion for the poll works
• And with the Vote Center Model
• Voters can go to any location in Navajo 
County and vote their ballot
• For example - If voters lives in Show Low 
but works in Holbrook, they can still go to 
the nearest vote center and vote
• We no longer have out of precinct 
provisional, literally eliminated that all 
together.
• The Navajo Dept. of Justice and praised 
Navajo County numerous times for our 
Vote Center Model, and even asked to 
help Apache County to get them on the 
model.

<!-- page 96 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
A RESOLUTION OF THE BOARD OF SUPERVISORS OF NAVAJO 
COUNTY, ARIZONA, ADOPTING A NEW VOTING PRECINCT MAP 
AND POLLING PLACES PURSUANT TO A.R.S. § 16-411 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 16-411 requires the Board of Supervisors to meet on or before 
October 1 of each year preceding the year of a general election for the purpose of establishing a 
convenient number of election precincts in the county and defining the boundaries of those 
precincts; and 
WHEREAS,  pursuant to A.R.S. 11-212 and A.R.S. 15-1441 (A), the Board on March 
22, 2022 adopted new Supervisorial District boundaries and Northland Pioneer Community 
Collage Precincts; and  
WHEREAS, the County has a need and desire to make improvements to and simplify its 
existing election precinct maps and boundaries  
WHEREAS, County staff have made recommendations to the Board regarding new 
precinct lines that would (1) be consistent with the new redistricting plans adopted by the 
County, (2) eliminate voter confusion, (3) enhance the efficient use of County election resources, 
(4) accomplish all of the foregoing without any significant inconvenient to any group of voters in
the County.
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Board as follows: 
1.)  That a new precinct map and updated boundaries attached hereto and incorporated 
herein as “Exhibit A.”  are hereby approved by the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors. 
2.)  The polling places and/or voting centers to be utilized for each of these precincts will 
be established pursuant to procedures set forth A.R.S. §16-411(B).

Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approving the renewal of an Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) with the
Navajo Nation and Navajo County. This IGA has an attached task agreement (Attachment A) to
fund two equipment operators in the amount of $120,000.00 per year for road maintenance
projects for public roads within Navajo County. 
Background:
Navajo Nation and Navajo County have agreed that in order to continue to assist Navajo Nation
and BIA to maintain public roads within Navajo County in an efficient manner, the equipment
operator must be part of the Navajo Division of Transportation (NDOT). Navajo County has
agreed to fund two equipment operators to assist Navajo Nation and BIA for road maintenance
of Public roads within District One and District Two of Navajo County. The allocated funds
will be used as a reimbursement to NDOT for the payment of salary and benefits of two
NDOT equipment operators. The equipment operators will perform maintenance work only on
BIA and NDOT routes within the boundaries of Navajo County District One and Two. 
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval of the Intergovernmental Agreement. 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2024
Budgeted Y/N: Y
Amount Requested: $120,000.0
Fiscal Impact:
FY 2024 Special Road Funds 
District 1 - $60,000.00
District 2 - $60,000.00

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN THE NAVAJO NATION 
AND 
NAVAJO COUNTY, ARIZONA 
 
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT ("IGA") is made and entered into by and 
between the Navajo Nation, acting through its Navajo Division of Transportation (the 
"NATION"), and Navajo County, a political subdivision of the State of Arizona, acting through 
its Public Works Department (the "COUNTY"). 
 
WHEREAS, by the adoption of Resources and Development Committee Resolutions the 
NATION has previously approved road maintenance projects for certain public roads within 
Navajo County and the Navajo Nation and may from time to time approve additional such 
projects (all such projects to be collectively referred to herein as the "PROJECTS"); and 
 
WHEREAS, the NATION is a sovereign entity and has the inherent power to plan and 
develop an integrated transportation network of highways, roads, waterways, airports, 
railroads and pipelines; and 
 
WHEREAS, the COUNTY is authorized by A.R.S §11 -952 to enter into intergovernmental 
agreements for joint or cooperative action with agencies of the United States and/or Tribal 
governments; and 
 
WHEREAS, the NATION and the COUNTY have previously entered into a Memorandum of 
Understanding MOU #223 dated March 15, 2023 (the "MOU"), expressing their intent to 
cooperate in transportation-related projects for their mutual benefit; and 
 
WHEREAS, consistent with the MOU, the COUNTY has agreed to assist the NATION and 
individual Chapters of the NATION by acting as their agent for the procurement of materials 
and supplies required to complete the PROJECTS, providing project planning support, and 
as agreed on a project -by-project basis to provide labor and equipment to assist in road 
repair activity for PROJECTS which are eligible for such activity by the COUNTY under the 
applicable Arizona statutes and constitutional provisions, 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the parties hereby agree as follows: 
 
1. Purpose. The purpose of this IGA is for the COUNTY to assist the NATION and 
individual Chapters of the NATION by acting as their agent for the procurement of road 
materials and supplies for the PROJECTS; providing project planning assistance to the 
Nation and the Chapters in connection with the PROJECTS; and, as may be agreed by 
Navajo County on a project-by-project basis, assisting in road repair activities which are 
eligible for such activity by the COUNTY under the applicable Arizona statutes and 
constitutional provisions. All other work in connection with the PROJECTS, including but 
not limited to permitting, shall be the responsibility of the NATION.

<!-- page 105 -->
Navajo Nation/Navajo County IGA for Roads Projects

Page 2 of 7 
 
 
 
 
Unless otherwise agreed to in writing, the COUNTY shall have no responsibility for the 
maintenance or repair of the roads after the completion of a PROJECT. 
 
1.1 Initial and Additional PROJECTS . The parties may agree to additional 
PROJECTS, each such additional PROJECTS to be described in a separate 
Attachment to be appended to this IGA with the written approval of the 
authorized representatives of both parties. Any such additional PROJECT shall 
be governed by the terms of this IGA. 
 
2. Funding. For each additional PROJECT as described in Section 1.1 above, the 
funding source for such PROJECT shall be set forth in the Attachment describing that 
PROJECT. 
 
3. Appropriations. The sum referenced in Section 2 above has been set aside for the 
purposes set forth in Section 2 and Attachment A of this agreement. 
 
4. Key Officials. The key officials for each party are as follows: 
 
Public Works Director 
Navajo County 
Public Works Department

5. Need for Materials, Supplies, Road Repair Activity and Project Planning  
Support. The parties shall jointly agree on the materials and supplies required for the 
PROJECTS before each procurement is made. The COUNTY shall agree to the level 
and type of support it will provide to assist the NATION on the road repair activity. The 
COUNTY will provide project planning assistance and guidance in determining what  
materials and supplies are required. Additional project planning support may be 
provided by the COUNTY as requested by the NATION, but it is understood that the 
completion of the PROJECTS is the responsibility of the NATION and/or the Chapters 
which will benefit from the PROJECTS. If the COUNTY will be acting in any capacity 
other than that of a procurement agent in connection with a PROJECT, the NATION 
and/or Chapters shall provide such documentation as the COUNTY may reasonably 
require that the PROJECT roads are eligible for expenditures of COUNTY  resources 
under the applicable Arizona statutes and constitutional provisions. The NATION shall 
provide this document, duly executed, when projects require services in addition to 
procurement.

<!-- page 106 -->
Navajo Nation/Navajo County IGA for Roads Projects 
Effective date of IGA is . 
Page 3 of 7 
 
 
 
 
 
6. Procurement Procedures . Unless otherwise agreed in writing by the parties, all 
materials and supplies procured by the COUNTY pursuant to this IGA shall be 
procured in accordance with the Arizona Procurement Code and the Navajo County 
Procurement Policy.  Procurement shall include the delivery of the materials and 
supplies by the vendor to the site(s) where they are required. 
 
7. Payment to the COUNTY. 
 
A.  The NATION shall advance funding for each PROJECT prior to any material being 
ordered. For materials, supplies, equipment and labor, the NATION shall pay the 
COUNTY for the actual cost and any directly related procurement expenses 
(newspaper ads, etc.) The COUNTY shall not charge the NATION any overhead 
or administrative expenses for procurement and project planning services under 
this IGA. If the advanced funds are not adequate to complete the PROJECT for 
any reason, the NATION shall advance additional funds within 30 days after receipt 
of a written request by the COUNTY; the COUNTY shall have no obligation to 
continue with any PROJECT for which adequate funding has not been received. 
 
B.  The COUNTY will provide a quarterly report showing expenditures against the 
advance. 
 
C. Each quarterly report shall reference this IGA and the appropriate Attachment. 
 
D.  The COUNTY shall provide for strict accountability of all costs of materials, 
supplies, equipment and labor applied against the advance, shall maintain an 
accurate accounting of all PROJECT-related expenditures, and shall preserve such 
records for at least three (3) years following the final payment under this IGA. 
Copies of all correspondence, reports and invoices associated with this IGA 
shall be furnished to the NATION. 
 
8. Payment to the NATION. 
 
A. It is understood and agreed by the Parties that the COUNTY's contributions 
towards the Additional PROJECTS are to be paid out of the Fund from HURF funds 
allocated to District 1 and 2. 
B. It is specifically and expressly understood and agreed that the amount which the 
COUNTY is required to contribute is directly linked amount contributed from HURF 
funds allocated to District 1 & 2 may be reduced due to economic downturns. 
 
9. No Surplus Funds. Upon completion of each PROJECT and at the termination of 
this IGA, there shall be no surplus money on hand because all funds advanced will 
have been disbursed on PROJECT expenditures or returned to the NATION. The 
COUNTY shall refund surplus funds to the NATION 30 days after parties mutually 
agree a PROJECT is complete.

<!-- page 107 -->
Navajo Nation/Navajo County IGA for Roads Projects

Page 4 of 7 
 
 
 
10. Assignment. The COUNTY shall not assign or transfer any interest in this IGA or 
assign any claims for reimbursement under this IGA without the prior written approval 
of the NATION.  The COUNTY shall not assign any of its responsibilities under this 
IGA without the prior written approval of the NATION. 
 
11. Term. This IGA shall not become effective until executed by both the President of the 
Navajo Nation and the Chairman of the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. This 
IGA shall terminate 10 years after the date of the last signature. 
 
12.  Amendment / Renewal. This IGA shall not be altered, changed or amended except 
by a written instrument executed by both parties. On or before the scheduled date of 
termination, this IGA may be renewed for one or more additional years with the written 
concurrence of both parties. 
 
13. Termination. Either party may terminate this IGA, or its participation in any PROJECT, 
upon written notice if the other party has failed to cure a default here under thirty (30) 
days after written notice of default from the non-defaulting party. Upon such 
termination, reimbursement, not covered by the advance, shall be made for materials 
and supplies procured and project planning support provided prior to the date of 
termination. Money advanced to the COUNTY but not spent or committed shall be 
refunded to the NATION prior to the date of termination. 
 
14.  Scope of Agreement. This IGA incorporates all of the agreements, covenants and 
understandings between the parties hereto concerning the subject matter hereof, and 
all such covenants, agreements and understandings have been merged into this IGA. 
No prior agreement or understanding, oral or otherwise, of the parties or their agents 
shall be valid or enforceable unless embodied in this IGA. 
 
15. Severability. In the event that any portion of this IGA is determined to be void, 
unconstitutional or otherwise unenforceable, the remainder of this IGA shall remain in 
full force and effect. 
 
 
16. Other Terms and Conditions. 
 
A. Nothing in this IGA is intended to, nor shall it be interpreted to, grant or alter any 
existing jurisdiction of any government over the NATION's public roads.  The 
COUNTY's participation in this IGA is in the spirit of cooperation described in the 
MOU, and, except  as expressly set forth  herein, the COUNTY shall have no 
authority, responsibility or liability in connection with the PROJECTS or the roads 
that are the subject of the PROJECTS, including road deterioration as a result of 
road use

B. Neither the NATION nor the COUNTY waives any rights (including but not limited 
to treaty rights, immunities, sovereign  immunities, jurisdictional defenses, or 
defenses based on their respective protecting laws). Specifically, nothing in this 
IGA shall be construed as a waiver of sovereign immunity by the NATION, except 
as provided for in the Navajo Sovereign Immunity Act, as amended, at 1 N.N.C. 
§§ 551, et seq. Nothing herein shall constitute the NATION's consent to be sued, 
or consent to jurisdiction of any federal or state court. Nothing in this IGA creates, 
implies or shall be construed to create any right of action in any third party. 
 
C. Nothing in this IGA is intended to, or shall be interpreted to, allocate or shift any 
current or future liability of a party to this IGA due to such party's acts or omissions 
to the other party to this IGA. 
 
D. Each party to this IGA is responsible for its own negligent acts and omissions of its 
employees and agents. The Navajo Nation acknowledges that it will be responsible 
for the claim of damages arising from personal injury or damages to person or 
property to the extent they result from the negligence of the tribal employees. The 
liability of Navajo Nation shall be subject to the immunities and limitations prov ided 
for under the Navajo Nation Sovereign Immunity Act 1 N.N.C. §§ 551 et seq. 
 
17. Dispute Resolution Procedures. Any dispute arising out of or relating to this IGA, 
shall be resolved by arbitration as contemplated by the Navajo Nation Sovereign 
Immunity Act, 1 N.N.C. §§ 554(J) and (K), and the Navajo Nation Arbitration Act, 7 
N.N.C. §§ 1101 et seq.  The arbitration shall be conducted in accordance with the 
International Institute for Conflict Prevention and Resolution Rules for Non - 
Administered Arbitration. 
 
Notice of intent to invoke arbitration against the NATION shall be filed in compliance 
with the notice requirements of the Navajo Nation Sovereign Immunity Act, 1 N.N.C. 
§ 555.

related to the maintenance activities within the boundaries the Navajo County District I

31, 2026, wit h an option of renewal for 2 additional years. The work to be performed 
under Attachment A may be terminated at any time by mutual written consent, or by the 
County, in whole or in part, with or without cause, upon giving ninety (90) days written 
notice. If work performed under Attachment A is terminated, the County shall be liable 
only for payment under the payment provisions for services rendered and accepted 
material received by the County before the effective date of termination.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. b.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: Intergovernmental Agreement for the Bourdon Ranch Road & Roundup Drive Left
Turn Lane Project - Dist III
Submitted For: Madhav Mundle, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: Jeanine Carruthers, Deputy Director Administration
Department: Public Works
Presented By: Madhav Mundle
Motion before the Board:
Consideration of approval to enter into an Intergovernmental Agreement between the State of
Arizona and Navajo County for the Bourdon Ranch Road & Roundup Drive Left Turn Lane
Project.
Background:
The work proposed under this Intergovernmental Agreement (IGA) consists of the construction
of a left turn lane and installation of signing and striping at the intersection of Bourdon Ranch
Road and Roundup Drive, (the “Project”). This project is within the boundary of District 3. The
Arizona Department of Transportation (ADOT) has determined that Navajo County’s request to
utilize Highway Safety Improvement Program (HSIP) funds for a safety countermeasure project
to install a left-turn lane at the intersection of Bourdon Ranch Rd. and Roundup Dr., is eligible
for the HSIP funding. The funding for this project is established under the ADOT HSIP and is
eligible to be funded at 94.3% HSIP federal funds and 5.7% local match as described in Code of
Federal Register 23 CFR Part 924.  ADOT has determined that, in accordance with 23 USC
148(a)(4))(A), this project is consistent with the State's 2019 Strategic Highway Safety Plan. It
supports the Intersections emphasis area by implementing the infrastructure upgrade or
improvement strategy.
The Project cost, shown in Exhibit A of the IGA, is estimated at $1,032,838.00, which includes
federal aid and the Navajo County’s (Local Agency) match. ADOT will administer the design
and advertise, bid and award, and administer the construction phase of the Project. The interest
of ADOT in this Project is the acquisition of federal funds for the use and benefit of the Local
Agency and authorization of such federal funds for the Project pursuant to federal law and
regulations. The Navajo County’s match for this Project is 5.7%, which will be utilized for
ADOT Project Development Administration (PDA) Cost, Design and Construction Cost. The
total estimated cost for Navajo County for this Project is $ 58,872.00.
 
Recommendation: 
Staff recommends approval.

<!-- page 114 -->
Staff recommends approval.

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT 
 
BETWEEN 
THE STATE OF ARIZONA 
AND 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
 
 
THIS AGREEMENT (“Agreement”) is entered into this date ________________________________, pursuant 
to the Arizona Revised Statutes (“A.R.S.”) §§ 11-951 through 11-954, as amended, between the 
STATE OF ARIZONA, acting by and through its DEPARTMENT OF TRANSPORTATION (the “State” or 
“ADOT”) and the NAVAJO COUNTY, acting by and through its CHAIRMAN and 
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS (the “County” or “Local Agency”). The State and the Local Agency are each 
individually referred to as a “Party” and are collectively referred to as the “Parties.” 
 
 
I. RECITALS 
 
1. The State is empowered by A.R.S. § 28-401 to enter into this Agreement and has delegated 
to the undersigned the authority to execute this Agreement on behalf of the State. 
 
2. The Local Agency  is empowered by A.R.S. § 11-251 to enter into this Agreement and has by 
resolution, if required, a copy of which is attached and made a part of, resolved to enter into 
this Agreement and has authorized the undersigned to execute this Agreement on behalf of 
the Local Agency. 
 
3. The work proposed under this Agreement consists of the construction of a left turn lane and 
installation of signing and striping at the intersection of Bourdon Ranch Road and Roundup 
Drive, (the “Project”). The Project cost, shown in Exhibit A, is estimated at $1,032,838.00, 
which includes federal aid and the Local Agency‘s match. The State will administer the 
design and advertise, bid and award, and administer the construction phase of the Project. 
 
4. The interest of the State in this Project is the acquisition of federal funds for the use and 
benefit of the Local Agency and authorization of such federal funds for the Project pursuant 
to federal law and regulations. The State shall be the designated agent for the Local Agency 
for the Project, if the Project is approved by Federal Highway Administration (FHWA) and 
funds for the Project are available.

<!-- page 116 -->
IGA 23-0009378-I 
 
 
Page 2 of 11 
 
5. The foregoing Recitals and Exhibit A shall be incorporated into this Agreement. 
In consideration of the mutual terms expressed herein, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
 
 
II. SCOPE OF WORK 
 
1. The Parties agree:  
 
a. The Project will be completed, accepted, and paid for in accordance with the 
requirements of the Project plans and specifications. 
 
b. The final cost estimate may exceed the initial estimate identified in Exhibit A, and in 
such case, the Local Agency is responsible for and agrees to pay, the difference prior to 
bid advertisement. 
 
c. The final Project amount may exceed the initial estimate(s) identified in Exhibit A, and 
in such case, the Local Agency is responsible for, and agrees to pay, any and all actual 
costs exceeding the initial estimate. If the final Project amount is less than the initial 
estimate, the difference between the final bid amount and the initial estimate will be de-
obligated or otherwise released from the Project and the federal aid returned to the 
State. The Local Agency acknowledges it remains responsible for actual costs and agrees 
to pay according to the terms of this Agreement. 
 
d. The Local Agency and ADOT will each separately file a Notice of Intent (NOI) under the 
Construction General Permit (CGP) with the Arizona Department of Environmental 
Quality (ADEQ) before construction begins, if applicable to the Project. 
 
 2. The State will: 
 
a. Execute this Agreement, and if the Project is approved by FHWA and funds for the 
Project are available, be the Local Agency’s designated agent for the Project. 
 
b. After this Agreement is executed, and prior to performing or authorizing any work, 
invoice the Local Agency  for the Local Agency’s share of the initial Project Development 
Administration (PDA) costs, estimated at $1,710.00 and the Local Agency’s share of the 
Project design costs, estimated at $18,022.00. If PDA costs exceed the estimate during 
the development of design, notify the Local Agency, obtain concurrence prior to 
continuing with the development of design, and invoice as determined by ADOT and the 
Local Agency for additional costs to complete PDA for the Project. If design costs exceed 
the estimate prior to completion of design, invoice the Local Agency for Project costs 
exceeding design. After the Project costs are finalized, invoice or reimburse the Local 
Agency for the difference between actual costs and the amount the Local Agency has 
paid for PDA and design.

<!-- page 117 -->
IGA 23-0009378-I 
 
 
Page 3 of 11 
 
c. After receipt of the PDA costs and the Local Agency’s estimated share of the Project 
design costs, submit all required documentation pertaining to the Project to FHWA with 
the recommendation that the maximum federal funds programmed for this Project be 
approved for scoping/design. After receipt of FHWA authorization, proceed to advertise 
for and enter into contract(s) with the consultant(s) for the design and post-design of 
the Project. Should costs exceed the maximum federal funds available it is understood 
and agreed that the Local Agency will be responsible for any overage. 
 
d. On behalf of the Local Agency, prepare and provide all documents pertaining to the 
design and post-design of the Project, incorporating comments from the Local Agency, 
as appropriate. Review and approve documents required by FHWA to qualify the 
Project for and to receive federal funds. Perform tasks that may consist of, but are not 
limited to, preparation of environmental documents; analysis and documentation of 
environmental categorical exclusion determinations; geologic materials testing and 
analysis; right of way related oversight and stewardship activities; preparation of 
reports, design plans, maps, specifications and cost estimates and other related tasks 
essential to the design development of the Project. 
 
e. After completion of design and prior to bid advertisement, invoice the Local Agency for 
the actual PDA costs, as applicable, and the Local Agency’s share of the Project 
construction costs, estimated at $39,140.00. After the Project costs for construction are 
finalized, the State will either invoice or reimburse the Local Agency for the difference 
between estimated and actual costs. De-obligate or otherwise release any remaining 
federal funds from the scoping/design phase of the Project. 
 
f. After receipt of the actual PDA costs, if applicable, and the Local Agency’s estimated 
share of the Project construction costs, including the difference between the final and 
the initial construction cost estimates, if applicable, submit all required documentation 
to FHWA with the recommendation that the maximum federal funds programmed for 
construction of this Project be approved. Should costs exceed the maximum federal 
funds available, it is understood and agreed that the Local Agency will be responsible 
for any overage. 
 
g. After receipt of FHWA authorization, proceed to advertise for, receive and open bids, 
award and enter into a contract with the firm for the construction of the Project. If the 
bid amounts exceed the construction cost estimate, obtain the Local Agency’s 
concurrence and invoice the Local Agency  for the difference between the construction 
cost estimate and the bid amount prior to awarding the contract.   
 
h. Notify the Local Agency of completion and final acceptance of the Project. At such time, 
file a Notice of Termination (NOT) with ADEQ transferring CGP responsibilities to the 
Local Agency, and provide a copy to the Local Agency indicating that the State’s 
maintenance responsibility of the Project is terminated, as applicable. 
 
i. Notify the Local Agency of completion and final acceptance of the Project; coordinate 
with the Local Agency and turn over full responsibility of the Project improvements.  
 
j. Not be obligated to maintain the Project, should the Local Agency fail to budget or 
provide for proper and perpetual maintenance as set forth in this Agreement.

3. The Local Agency will:

design costs, estimated at $18,022.00. Agree to be responsible for actual PDA costs, if 
during the development of design, PDA costs exceed the initial estimate. Be responsible 
and pay for the difference between the estimated and actual PDA and design costs of the 
Project within 30 days of receipt of an invoice. 
 
c. Review design plans, specifications, cost estimates and other such documents required 
for the construction bidding and construction of the Project, including scoping/design 
plans and documents required by FHWA to qualify projects for and to receive federal 
funds; provide design review comments to the State as appropriate.   
 
d. After completion of design, within 30 days of receipt of an invoice from the State and 
prior to bid advertisement, pay to the State any outstanding PDA costs,  the Local 
Agency’s share of the Project construction costs, estimated at $39,140.00, and if 
applicable, the difference between the final and initial construction cost estimates.  Be 
responsible for and pay the difference between the estimated construction cost and 
Project bid amount prior to award. After Project completion, be responsible for and pay 
any outstanding Project costs, within 30 days of receipt of an invoice. 
 
e. Be responsible for all costs incurred in performing and accomplishing the work as set 
forth under this Agreement, that are not covered by federal funding. Should costs be 
deemed ineligible or exceed the maximum federal funds available, it is understood and

shall be made within 30 days of receipt of an invoice from the State.

III. MISCELLANEOUS PROVISIONS 
 
1. Effective Date. This Agreement shall become effective upon signing and dating of all Parties. 
 
2. Amendments. Any change or modification to the Project will only occur with the mutual 
written consent of both Parties. 
 
3. Duration. The terms, conditions and provisions of this Agreement shall remain in full force 
and effect until completion of the Project and all related deposits and/or reimbursements 
are made. Any and all obligations of maintenance hereunder shall remain perpetual and 
shall survive any termination hereof and the assignment or assumption of this Agreement 
or the Project by another competent jurisdiction or entity.

<!-- page 120 -->
IGA 23-0009378-I 
 
 
Page 6 of 11 
 
4. Cancellation. This Agreement may be cancelled at any time up to 30 days before the award 
of the Project contract, so long as the cancelling Party provides at least 30 days’ prior 
written notice to the other Party. It is understood and agreed that, in the event the Local 
Agency terminates this Agreement, the Local Agency shall be responsible for all costs 
incurred by the State up to the time of termination. It is further understood and agreed that 
in the event the Local Agency terminates this Agreement, the State shall in no way be 
obligated to complete or maintain the Project. 
 
5. Indemnification. The Local Agency shall indemnify, defend, and hold harmless the State, any 
of its departments, agencies, boards, commissions, officers or employees (collectively 
referred to in this paragraph as the “State”) from any and all claims, demands, suits, actions, 
proceedings, loss, cost and damages of every kind and description, including reasonable 
attorneys' fees and/or litigation expenses (collectively referred to in this paragraph as the 
“Claims”), which may be brought or made against or incurred by the State on account of loss 
of or damage to any property or for injuries to or death of any person, to the extent caused 
by, arising out of, or contributed to, by reasons of any alleged act, omission, professional 
error, fault, mistake, or negligence of the Local Agency, its employees, officers, directors, 
agents, representatives, or contractors, their employees, agents, or representatives in 
connection with or incident to the performance of this Agreement. The Local Agency’s 
obligations under this paragraph shall not extend to any Claims to the extent caused by the 
negligence of the State, except the obligation does apply to any negligence of the Local 
Agency which may be legally imputed to the State by virtue of the State’s ownership or 
possession of land. The Local Agency’s obligations under this paragraph shall survive the 
termination of this Agreement. 
 
6. Third-Party Indemnification. The State shall include Section 107.13 of the 2021 version of 
the Arizona Department of Transportation Standard Specifications for Road and Bridge 
Construction, incorporated into this Agreement by reference, in the State’s contract with 
any and all contractors, of which the Local Agency shall be specifically named as a third-
party beneficiary. This provision may not be amended without the approval of the Local 
Agency.  
 
7. Programmed Federal Funds. The cost of scoping, design, construction and construction 
engineering work under this Agreement is to be covered by the federal funds programmed 
for this Project, up to the maximum available. The Local Agency acknowledges that actual 
Project costs may exceed the maximum available amount of federal funds, or that certain 
costs may not be accepted by FHWA as eligible for federal funds. Therefore, the Local 
Agency agrees to pay the difference between actual costs of the Project and the federal 
funds received.  
 
8. Termination of Federal Funding. Should the federal funding related to this Project be 
terminated or reduced by the federal government, or Congress rescinds, fails to renew, or 
otherwise reduces apportionments or obligation authority, the State shall in no way be 
obligated for funding or liable for any past, current or future expenses under this 
Agreement. 
 
9. Indirect Costs. The cost of the Project under this Agreement includes indirect costs 
approved by FHWA, as applicable.

and ordinances, as may be amended.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. c. 1.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: SUP 23-009 AZ11-153 Rooster Wireless Communication Facility - District III
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planning Manager
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration of the adoption of Resolution
35-2023 approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 80’
monopole wireless communication tower and associated facilities on a vacant, 13.15-acre parcel,
in the White Mountain Lakes area.
Background:
The subject parcel was created, legally, by an Assessor’s Office Split in April of 2023.  Though
not required, all resulting parcels of this split met the requirements of Planning and
Development Services’ Plat Alteration process.  
At the Thursday, August 17th, 2023 Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing,
the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended denial of this
project.
 
Recommendation: 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special
Use Permit, subject to the conditions contained within the Staff Report.

DEVELOPMENT NARRATIVE REPORT 
PROJECT NAME:  AZ11-153 Rooster
APPLICANT: Sunstate Towers on behalf of Verizon Wireless 
REQUEST: Special Use Permit for new Wireless 
Communications Facility located at 1911 Bourdon Ranch Rd. 
Show Low, AZ 85901 
34.3529536, -109.9596655

<!-- page 131 -->
2 
Purpose of the Request 
This WCF is needed to provide the latest service to cellular and data customers in the 
area for Verizon Wireless. As seen on the adjoining RF Justification document, this 
tower will fill gaps and give the area a much-needed boost in quality, reliability and 
strength of signal both in and outdoors.  
Description of Proposal 
This WCF will provide wireless communication and data services to Verizon 
customers. The WCF is an unmanned facility that will run 24 hours, 7 days a week, 
365 days a year. There will be a technician at the site once a month to monitor the 
installation and will be built on a vacant lot where it will follow the County's latest zoning 
standards.
This proposal is for a 80' monopole tower with Verizon Wireless antennas at a 
centerline of 76' and an opportunity for co-location of additional carriers in the future. 
The leased area will be surrounded by a 8' tall chain link fence. 
Relationship to Surrounding Properties 
Enhancing the surrounding area with an upgraded wireless communications facility will 
provide consistent coverage with minimal imprint on the surroundings. There are no 
colocation opportunities that will service Verizon’s needs in the area. Please refer to 
the provided RF Justification document for further details. 
The facility shall have few, if any impacts, beyond providing service to the Verizon 
Wireless customers. There should be no discernible impacts to pedestrian or vehicular 
transit, and the facility will not emit odor, dust, gas, noise, vibration, smoke, heat or 
glare. Beyond a once-a-month operations inspection, the site should generate no 
activity or impact, and will not utilize connections to water, sewe r or refuse 
utilities/services. The following suggested sections are, to a large degree, not 
applicable: 
LOCATION AND ACCESSIBILITY- See drawings/survey and exhibits. 
CIRCULATION SYSTEM – There will be no effect on traffic.

<!-- page 132 -->
3 
DEVELOPMENT SCHEDULE- Applicant intends to construct the improvement as soon 
as all permits have been received. 
COMM
UNITY FACILITIES AND SERVICES- Facility will support wireless 
communications in the community. 
PUBLI
C UTILITIES AND SERVICES- Power and telco access are the only utilities 
required by the facility.

file:///C/...20Eye)%20-%20AZ3%20Bourdon%20Ranch%20Rd/Zoning/Neighborhood%20meeting/Neighborhood%20meeting%20notes.txt[6/8/2023 11:47:17 AM]
6/7/2023
AZ11-153 Rooster Neighborhood meeting notes:
Started meeting via pre determined Zoom link at 5:50pm
Attendees:
Steven Kennedy
Kathy/Buz
Tony Tangalos
Dave
Elizabeth Classen
-Presented Agenda
-RF engineer presented RF Power Point Presentation
Opened the meeting to questions
Tony:
-Doesn't feel tower is needed for coverage for his needs as airport owner as he "has at least 2 bars" which is sufficient 
for him to make calls.
-Reiterated to Tony and rest of attendee's of FAA and FCC approvals as well as Navajo County stipulations for 
zoning/permitting approval needed to proceed with the project
-Asked about SWF's and included Buzz/Kathy-RF explained
Buzz/Kathy 
-Concerned about multiple towers in the area and doesn't think it's needed
-Wants us to inquire about the other tower proposed approximately 1 mile to the west by reg destry in Scottsdale- will 
they build both?
Advised I have taken notes and will inquire about the other proposed tower by the neighborhood.
Ended meeting at approximately 7:19pm

<!-- page 201 -->
WIRELESS NETWORK CONSULTING
RF DESIGN ANALYSIS
AZ11-153 Rooster / Verizon AZ3_BOURDON-RANCH
Coverage Cell Split
2023

<!-- page 202 -->
2023
Coverage vs Capacity
Capacity is providing bandwidth or processing 
capacity to service the customers in the area.
– Areas where large numbers of users are in a specific 
geographic areas
– Areas where users are demanding higher data rates 
for services
– Areas with a large amount of indoor users
Coverage is Providing Service where service 
does not exist, calls drop, or “no service”.
– Areas where sites are farther apart
– Areas where terrain or buildings block signals
– Areas where indoor service is low or nonexistent

<!-- page 203 -->
2023
Objective of new site
Capacity
– Provide additional bandwidth for customers in the area 
surrounding the proposed site
– Provide better throughput for indoor users in the area
– Offload surrounding sites in area.
Coverage
– Provide additional coverage on the East side of White Mountain 
Lakes Estates
– Provide coverage at the Silver Creek Golf Club
– Provide coverage along Bourdon Ranch Rd and surrounding 
communities
Why is this site important?
– 96% of Americans own a Cellular Phone
– 57% of American Homes rely exclusively on cellular phones 
– 84% or more of 9-1-1 emergency calls are made from wireless 
devices

<!-- page 204 -->
2023
Proposed Site
80’ Monopole Tower
– 1911 Bourdon Ranch Rd. Show Low, AZ 85901
• Latitude:     34.3529536 N (NAD83)
• Longitude: -109.9596655 W (NAD83)
• Ground Elevation: 6097.9' (NAVD88)
• Anchor tenant is Verizon
– Antenna Centerline at 76’ AGL

<!-- page 205 -->
2023
Why here?
Lack of service along Bourdon Ranch Road and 
connector roads.
Lack of service at the White Mountain Lake 
Airpark
Lack of indoor services in the surrounding 
suburban/rural developments
Significant amount of user increase in the rural
areas of Arizona
Most of the users' mobiles are reporting low 
quality connections

<!-- page 206 -->
2023
Sites
7.47 miles
3.84 miles
2.72 miles

<!-- page 207 -->
2023

<!-- page 212 -->

Best Server - Current (-120dbm)

<!-- page 213 -->
2023
Best Server – with new site 
(-120dbm)

<!-- page 214 -->
2023
Ionizing vs Non-Ionizing
There are two (2) types of Energy/Radio Waves
– Ionizing
• These are waves that can effect human DNA
• Examples are:
– Gamma rays
– X-Rays
• This is one of the reasons the nurse steps out of the room and you wear a lead overcoat 
when you get X-Rays at the dentist.
– Non- Ionizing
• These are waves do not effect human DNA
• Examples are:
– Car Radios
– Television 
– Wi-Fi Access points and routers
– Bluetooth headsets
– Cellphones and Smartphones
– Lightbulbs
– Wireless Baby Monitors
– TV remotes
– Absorption of waves is proximity based, the closer you are to the antenna the more non-
ionizing energy is absorbed.  You will absorb 50% of the FCC’s General Public limit with your 
smartphone next to your ear versus less than 10% of the FCC’s General Public limit from the 
antennas when you are standing 20’ away from the proposed tower. 
– The further you walk away from the tower it decreases even more.

<!-- page 215 -->
2023
General Public & Occupational limits
The FCC isolated two (2) groups relative to access around wireless antennas
The first group is called Occupational
– This refers to areas where workers would be allowed (general public cannot access) but the workers would 
not have knowledge about antennas (An example would be an Air Conditioner Repair Technician).  Barriers 
or signage may be needed to alert the worker when close to the antennas.
• Examples are:  
– Rooftop access behind a locked door
– Compound access behind a locked gate
– The FCC determined the safe value and then lowered by a factor of 10 and that is the value the 
wireless carriers use in the studies
– The exposure levels are averaged over 6 minutes
The second group is called General Public
– Uncontrolled access (General Public) 
• This group is for areas with general public access, the public would not have a knowledge of an 
antenna being close to them
• Examples are:
– Sidewalks
– Parks
– Public accessed buildings
– The FCC determined the safe value and then lowered by a factor of 50 and that is the value the 
wireless carriers use in the studies
– The exposure levels are averaged over 30 minutes
Compare the value for a tower which is 10mW to the power of a 
smartphone which is 200mW of power.

<!-- page 216 -->
Power Levels below a tower
• Power levels on the 
ground around the 
tower are much less 
than what is at the 
antennas
• Power on the ground 
adjacent to the tower 
is 1/1000 of the 
power compared to 
what is at the antenna
Main beam of the 
antenna
1/1000 of the power 
on the ground 
around the site

General Notes
1. The contractor and manufacturer shall verify all dimensions prior to fabrication
or construction and notify engineer of any noted discrepancy.
2. Typical details apply unless noted otherwise.  
3. Drawings and calculations represent the finished conditions upon completion
of construction.  Contractor is responsible for means and methods including 
rigging, shoring, temporary bracing, etc. as required to complete construction.
4. Drawings and calculations apply only to members specifically designed herein.  
Structural Steel
1. Structural Steel shall conform to the following ASTM specifications:
Tapered Poles: A572, Grade 65
Baseplates (Up to 2" thick): A572, Grade 55
Baseplates (Over 2" thick): A572, Grade 50
Pipe: A53, Grade B (35 ksi)
Hollow Structural Sections: A500, Grade B (46 ksi)
Plate for Reinforced Ports: A572, Grade 65
All Other Shapes and Plates: A36, unless noted otherwise
Bolts (High Strength): F3125, Grade A325
Anchor Bolts: A615, Grade 75 (as noted)
F1554, Grade 55 
2. High strength bolts shall be fully tensioned by turn-of-nut or 
other AISC approved method.
3. Welding shall be performed by welders certified in the process and position required
in accordance with AWS D1.1 using 70 ksi electrodes, unless noted otherwise.
4. All steel shall be hot dipped galvanized in accordance with ASTM A123.

The tower is a monopole. 
This tower is designed using the TIA-222-H standard. 
The following design criteria apply:  
 Tower base elevation above sea level: 6101.20 ft. 
 Basic wind speed of 115 mph. 
 Risk Category II. 
 Exposure Category C. 
 Simplified Topographic Factor Procedure for wind speed-up calculations is used. 
 Topographic Category: 1. 
 Crest Height: 0.00 ft. 
 Deflections calculated using a wind speed of 60 mph. 
 A non-linear (P-delta) analysis was used. 
 Pressures are calculated at each section. 
 Stress ratio used in pole design is 1. 
 Local bending stresses due to climbing loads, feed line supports, and appurtenance mounts are not considered.

Thrust force is assumed to be acting axially for all pile batter angles.

      reinforcement exceeds 0.005 while simultaneously compressive strain in 
      concrete more than 0.003. See ACI 318‐14, Section 21.2.3.
  Z = depth of tensile zone in concrete section is less than 10 percent of 
      section depth.
Bending Stiffness (EI) = Computed Bending Moment / Curvature.
Position of neutral axis is measured from edge of compression side of pile.

Note that the values of moment capacity in the table above are not 
factored by a strength reduction factor (phi‐factor).
In ACI 318, the value of the strength reduction factor depends on whether 
the transverse reinforcing steel bars are tied hoops (0.65) or spirals (0.75).
The above values should be multiplied by the appropriate strength reduction 
factor to compute ultimate moment capacity according to ACI 318, 
or the value required by the design standard being followed.

Load Type 1: Load 1 = Shear, V, lbs, and Load 2 = Moment, M, in‐lbs

NOTES & SPECIFICATIONS1
SPECIAL INSPECTIONS:
1. STEEL FABRICATION SHALL BE DONE ON THE PREMISES OF A
FABRICATOR REGISTERED AND APPROVED AS REQUIRED BY THE
BUILDING OFFICIAL TO PERFORM SUCH WORK WITHOUT SPECIAL
INSPECTION. ALTERNATIVELY, SPECIAL INSPECTION OF MATERIALS,
WELDING, AND FABRICATION PROCEDURES SHALL BE REQUIRED FOR
FABRICATION BY AN UNAPPROVED FABRICATOR.
2. NO FIELD WELDING SHALL BE PERMITTED
3. THE FOLLOWING SPECIAL INSPECTIONS SHALL BE REQUIRED PER
CHAPTER 17 OF THE BUILDING CODE:
·PERIODIC SPECIAL INSPECTION OF HIGH-STRENGTH BOLTING (WHEN
APPLICABLE)
·PERIODIC SPECIAL INSPECTION OF PLACEMENT OF REINFORCING STEEL
·CONTINUOUS SPECIAL INSPECTION OF ANCHOR BOLTS PRIOR TO AND
DURING CONCRETE PLACEMENT
·CONTINUOUS SPECIAL INSPECTION OF CONCRETE PLACEMENT
·CONTINUOUS SPECIAL INSPECTION OF DRILLING OPERATIONS FOR PIER
FOUNDATIONS
·CONTINUOUS SPECIAL INSPECTION TO VERIFY LOCATION, PLUMBNESS,
DIAMETER, AND LENGTH OF PIER FOUNDATIONS
4. SPECIAL INSPECTION IS NOT REQUIRED FOR WORK OF A MINOR NATURE
OR AS WARRANTED BY CONDITIONS IN THE JURISDICTION AS APPROVED
BY THE BUILDING OFFICIAL.  THUS, SPECIAL INSPECTION ITEMS ABOVE
MAY BE WAIVED AS DEEMED APPROPRIATE BY THE BUILDING OFFICIAL.
STRUCTURAL OBSERVATION:
NO STRUCTURAL OBSERVATION IS REQUIRED.
DISCLAIMERS:
1. ALL STRUCTURAL COMPONENTS TO BE CONNECTED TOGETHER SHALL
BE COMPLETELY FIT UP ON THE GROUND OR OTHERWISE VERIFIED FOR
COMPATIBILITY PRIOR TO LIFTING ANY COMPONENT INTO PLACE.
REPAIRS REQUIRED DUE TO FIT-UP OR CONNECTION COMPATIBILITY
PROBLEMS AFTER PARTIAL ERECTION ARE THE FINANCIAL
RESPONSIBILITY OF THE CONTRACTOR.
2. WHERE EFFECTIVE PROJECTED AREAS (EPA) ARE USED, IT IS THE
RESPONSIBILITY OF OTHERS TO VERIFY INSTALLED EQUIPMENT DOES
NOT EXCEED LISTED EPA.

GENERAL DESIGN NOTES:
STRUCTURAL DESIGN IS BASED ON THE 2018 EDITION
INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE WITH ALL APPLICABLE
LOCAL AMENDMENTS AND THE TIA-222-H STANDARD.
DESIGN LOADS:
WIND: WIND SPEED = 115 MPH (3-SEC GUST) PER THE ASCE 7-16
STANDARD
RISK CATEGORY / STRUCTURE CLASS: II
EXPOSURE:  C
TOPOGRAPHIC CATEGORY:  1
CREST HEIGHT:  0 FT
ELEVATION:  6100 FT ABOVE SEA LEVEL
ICE: NONE PER THE TIA-222-H STANDARD
SEISMIC:
IMPORTANCE FACTOR:  1.00
RISK CATEGORY / STRUCTURE CLASS: II
MAPPED SPECTRAL RESPONSE ACCELERATIONS:
SS = 0.251g,  S1 = 0.078g
SITE CLASS:  D
SPECTRAL RESPONSE COEFFICIENTS:
SDS = 0.267g,  SD1 = 0.124g
SEISMIC DESIGN CATEGORY:  B
BASIC SEISMIC-FORCE-RESISTING-SYSTEM:
TELECOMMUNICATION TOWER: STEEL POLE
SEISMIC RESPONSE COEFFICIENT, Cs:  0.052
SEISMIC BASE SHEAR, V:  1.20 K
RESPONSE MODIFICATION FACTOR, R:  1.5
ANALYSIS PROCEDURE:  EQUIVALENT LATERAL FORCE
STRUCTURAL STEEL:
1. POLYGONAL MONOPOLE SHAFT STEEL SHALL CONFORM w/
ASTM A572 GR. 65, U.N.O.
2. STEEL FOR BASEPLATES UP TO 2" THICK SHALL CONFORM
W/ ASTM A572 GR. 55.  STEEL FOR BASEPLATES OVER 2"
THICK SHALL CONFORM W/ ASTM A572 GR. 50.
3. ALL STEEL PIPE SHALL CONFORM w/ ASTM A53 GR. B (35
KSI), U.N.O.
4. ALL STEEL RECTANGULAR TUBES (HSS) SHALL CONFORM
w/ ASTM A500 GR. B (46 KSI), U.N.O.
5. REINFORCED PORT STEEL SHALL CONFORM w/ ASTM A572
GR. 65, U.N.O.
6. ALL OTHER STEEL SHAPES & PLATES SHALL CONFORM w/
ASTM A36, U.N.O.
7. ALL BOLTS FOR STEEL-TO-STEEL CONNECTIONS SHALL
CONFORM w/ ASTM F3125 GR. A325, U.N.O.
8. ALL ANCHOR BOLTS SHALL CONFORM w/ ASTM A615 GR. 75,
U.N.O.
9. ALL WELDING SHALL BE PERFORMED BY CERTIFIED
WELDERS IN ACCORDANCE w/ THE LATEST VERSION OF THE
AMERICAN WELDING SOCIETY AWS D1.1. STEEL WELDS
SHALL BE BY E70XX LOW HYDROGEN ELECTRODES, U.N.O.
10. ALL STEEL SURFACES SHALL BE GALVANIZED IN
ACCORDANCE w/ ASTM A123 AND ASTM F2329 STANDARDS.
11. ALL BOLTED CONNECTIONS SHALL BE TIGHTENED PER THE
"TURN-OF-NUT" METHOD AS DEFINED BY AISC.
BASE DESIGN REACTIONS:
MOMENT, M = 1291 K-FT (1.0 WIND)
SHEAR, V = 20 K (1.0 WIND)
AXIAL, P = 23 K (1.2 DEAD)

GENERAL DESIGN NOTES:
1. FOUNDATION DESIGN IS BASED UPON THE 2018 EDITION INTERNATIONAL BUILDING CODE AND ALL APPLICABLE LOCAL AMENDMENTS.
DESIGN LOADS:
1. THIS FOUNDATION IS DESIGNED TO SUPPORT THE 80'-0" TALL, MONOPOLE TOWER DESIGNED BY PINNACLE CONSULTING, INC. (JOB NO.
AZ11-153). 
2. FACTORED BASE REACTIONS PROVIDED BY TOWER DRAWINGS ARE:
a. MOMENT = 1291 FT-KIPS (1.0 WIND)
b. SHEAR = 20 KIPS (1.0 WIND)
c. AXIAL = 23 KIPS (1.2 DEAD)
FOUNDATION NOTES:
1. FOUNDATION IS DESIGNED UTILIZING TIA-222 REV. H ANNEX F-1 PRESUMPTIVE SOIL PARAMETERS.
2. ASSUMED SAND TYPE SOIL WITH =110 pcf,  =30°, and k = 35 pci PER TABLE F-1 OF TIA. VERIFY SOIL TYPE DURING CONSTRUCTION.
3. FOUNDATION DESIGN DOES NOT APPLY TO SOILS WITH WEAKER CHARACTERISTICS THAN NOTED ABOVE OR IN LOCATIONS WHERE
UNCOMPACTED, UNDOCUMENTED OR ORGANIC FILL EXIST.
4. IT IS THE OWNER AND CONTRACTOR'S RESPONSIBILITY TO VERIFY SOIL CONDITIONS MEET OR EXCEED ASSUMPTIONS NOTED HEREIN.
MATERIAL NOTES:
1. ANCHOR BOLTS:  ASTM A615, GRADE 75
2. STEEL PLATE (TEMPLATES):  ASTM A36 (OR BETTER) UNLESS NOTED OTHERWISE
3. CONCRETE:
a. 28 DAY COMPRESSIVE STRENGTH (f'c) OF 4000 PSI
b. TYPE II PORTLAND CEMENT
c. 3/4" MAXIMUM AGGREGATE SIZE
4. REINFORCING STEEL:  ASTM A615, GRADE 60
FOUNDATION SPECIAL INSPECTIONS:
1. SPECIAL INSPECTIONS ARE REQUIRED PER IBC CHAPTER 17 FOR THE FOLLOWING ITEMS:
a. PERIODIC INSPECTION OF REINFORCING STEEL TO VERIFY SIZE AND PLACEMENT.
b. PERIODIC INSPECTION OF ANCHOR BOLTS TO VERIFY SIZE AND PLACEMENT.
c. CONTINUOUS INSPECTION OF CONCRETE PLACEMENT TO VERIFY PROPER PLACEMENT TECHNIQUES.
GENERAL CONSTRUCTION NOTES:
1. CONCRETE CONSTRUCTION SHALL BE IN ACCORDANCE WITH ACI 301, "SPECIFICATIONS FOR STRUCTURAL CONCRETE", LATEST EDITION.
