General Notes
1. The contractor and manufacturer shall verify all dimensions prior to fabrication
or construction and notify engineer of any noted discrepancy.
2. Typical details apply unless noted otherwise.  
3. Drawings and calculations represent the finished conditions upon completion
of construction.  Contractor is responsible for means and methods including 
rigging, shoring, temporary bracing, etc. as required to complete construction.
4. Drawings and calculations apply only to members specifically designed herein.  
Structural Steel
1. Structural Steel shall conform to the following ASTM specifications:
Tapered Poles: A572, Grade 65
Baseplates (Up to 2" thick): A572, Grade 55
Baseplates (Over 2" thick): A572, Grade 50
Pipe: A53, Grade B (35 ksi)
Hollow Structural Sections: A500, Grade B (46 ksi)
Plate for Reinforced Ports: A572, Grade 65
All Other Shapes and Plates: A36, unless noted otherwise
Bolts (High Strength): F3125, Grade A325
Anchor Bolts: A615, Grade 75 (as noted)
F1554, Grade 55 
2. High strength bolts shall be fully tensioned by turn-of-nut or 
other AISC approved method.
3. Welding shall be performed by welders certified in the process and position required
in accordance with AWS D1.1 using 70 ksi electrodes, unless noted otherwise.
4. All steel shall be hot dipped galvanized in accordance with ASTM A123.

The tower is a monopole. 
This tower is designed using the TIA-222-H standard. 
The following design criteria apply:  
 Tower base elevation above sea level: 6101.20 ft. 
 Basic wind speed of 115 mph. 
 Risk Category II. 
 Exposure Category C. 
 Simplified Topographic Factor Procedure for wind speed-up calculations is used. 
 Topographic Category: 1. 
 Crest Height: 0.00 ft. 
 Deflections calculated using a wind speed of 60 mph. 
 A non-linear (P-delta) analysis was used. 
 Pressures are calculated at each section. 
 Stress ratio used in pole design is 1. 
 Local bending stresses due to climbing loads, feed line supports, and appurtenance mounts are not considered.

Computational Options:

 ‐ Static loading specified

Output Options:
 ‐ Output files use decimal points to denote decimal symbols.
 ‐ Values of pile‐head deflection, bending moment, shear force, and 
   soil reaction are printed for full length of pile.
 ‐ Printing Increment (nodal spacing of output points) = 1
 ‐ No p‐y curves to be computed and reported for user‐specified depths
 ‐ Print using narrow report formats
   (Note: Some output information is omitted from the narrow report formats)

Definitions of Run Messages and Notes:
‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐‐
  C = concrete in section has cracked in tension.
  Y = stress in reinforcing steel has reached yield stress.
  T = ACI 318 criteria for tension‐controlled section met, tensile strain in 
      reinforcement exceeds 0.005 while simultaneously compressive strain in 
      concrete more than 0.003. See ACI 318‐14, Section 21.2.3.
  Z = depth of tensile zone in concrete section is less than 10 percent of 
      section depth.
Bending Stiffness (EI) = Computed Bending Moment / Curvature.
Position of neutral axis is measured from edge of compression side of pile.
Compressive stresses and strains are positive in sign.
Tensile stresses and strains are negative in sign.

factored by a strength reduction factor (phi‐factor).
In ACI 318, the value of the strength reduction factor depends on whether 
the transverse reinforcing steel bars are tied hoops (0.65) or spirals (0.75).

factor to compute ultimate moment capacity according to ACI 318, 
or the value required by the design standard being followed.

** DETERMINATION OF NO HAZARD TO AIR NAVIGATION **
The Federal Aviation Administration has conducted an aeronautical study under the provisions of 49 U.S.C.,
Section 44718 and if applicable Title 14 of the Code of Federal Regulations, part 77, concerning:
Structure: Antenna Tower AZ11-153 ROOSTER
Location: Show Low, AZ
Latitude: 34-21-10.63N NAD 83
Longitude: 109-57-34.79W
Heights: 6098 feet site elevation (SE)
80 feet above ground level (AGL)
6178 feet above mean sea level (AMSL)
This aeronautical study revealed that the structure does not exceed obstruction standards and would not be a
hazard to air navigation provided the following condition(s), if any, is(are) met:
Based on this evaluation, marking and lighting are not necessary for aviation safety. However, if marking/
lighting are accomplished on a voluntary basis, we recommend it be installed in accordance with FAA Advisory
circular 70/7460-1 M.
This determination is based, in part, on the foregoing description which includes specific coordinates, heights,
frequency(ies) and power. Any changes in coordinates, heights, and frequencies or use of greater power, except
those frequencies specified in the Colo Void Clause Coalition; Antenna System Co-Location; Voluntary Best
Practices, will void this determination. Any future construction or alteration, including increase to heights,
power, or the addition of other transmitters, requires separate notice to the FAA.This determination includes all
previously filed frequencies and power for this structure.
If construction or alteration is dismantled or destroyed, you must submit notice to the FAA within 5 days after
the construction or alteration is dismantled or destroyed.
This determination does include temporary construction equipment such as cranes, derricks, etc., which may be
used during actual construction of the structure. However, this equipment shall not exceed the overall heights as

Case Description for ASN 2023-AWP-10402-OE
Proposed temporary crane on a new 80-foot tower. 100 feet overall
Part 77 Obstruction Standard(s) Exceeded and Aeronautical Impacts, if any:
Aeronautical study revealed that the temporary structure will not exceed any Part 77 obstruction standard.
Aeronautical study confirmed that the temporary structure will have no effect on any existing or proposed
arrival, departure or en route instrument/visual flight rules (IFR/VFR) operations or procedures. Additionally,
aeronautical study confirmed that the temporary structure will have no physical or electromagnetic effect on the
operation of air navigation and communications facilities and will not impact any airspace and routes used by
the military. Based on this aeronautical study, the FAA finds that the temporary structure will have no adverse
effect on air navigation and will not impact any aeronautical operations or procedures.
Based on this aeronautical study, the structure would not constitute a substantial adverse effect on aeronautical
operations or procedures because it will be temporary. The temporary structure would not be considered a
hazard to air navigation provided all of the conditions specified in this determination are strictly met.
Based on this evaluation, marking and lighting are not necessary for aviation safety. However, if marking/
lighting are accomplished on a voluntary basis, we recommend it be installed in accordance with FAA Advisory
circular 70/7460-1 M.
This determination expires on 01/12/2025 unless extended, revised, or terminated by the issuing office.
It is required that FAA Form 7460-2, Notice of Actual Construction or Alteration, be e-filed within 5 days after
the temporary structure is dismantled.
NOTE: REQUEST FOR EXTENSION OF THE EFFECTIVE PERIOD OF THIS DETERMINATION MUST
BE E-FILED AT LEAST 15 DAYS PRIOR TO THE EXPIRATION DATE. AFTER RE-EVALUATION
OF CURRENT OPERATIONS IN THE AREA OF THE STRUCTURE TO DETERMINE THAT NO
SIGNIFICANT AERONAUTICAL CHANGES HAVE OCCURRED, YOUR DETERMINATION MAY BE
ELIGIBLE FOR ONE EXTENSION OF THE EFFECTIVE PERIOD.

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: September 26, 2023 
Case No:  SUP 23-009 
Action:   Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner: Pinnacle Consulting/Richard C & Pauline G Brooksby 
Project Name: AZ11-153 Rooster Wireless Communication Facility 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of 
this Staff Report.  The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended 
denial of this Special Use Permit. 
 
Location: The property is located in the White Mountain Lakes area on APN # 304-36-003H. 
 
Size: 13.15 acres 
 
Supervisor District: III 
 
Zoning: R1-10 (Single-Family Residential Zoning District 10,000 Square Feet Per Dwelling Unit) 
 
Property Owners: Richard C and Pauline G Brooksby 
 
Applicant: Pinnacle Consulting 
 
Representative: John Katsiris 
 
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:  
A request for a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 80’ monopole wireless 
communication tower and associated facilit ies on a vacant, 13.15-acre parcel, in the White Mountain 
Lakes area. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
To the north, west, and east of the subject property are large tracts of vacant, R1-10 zoned property.  To 
the south of the property are further tracts of large R1-10 property, which appear to contain structures, 
though none of them are classified as legal residences within Navajo County’s records.

<!-- page 363 -->
Board of Supervisors              2 | P a g e  
 
 
BACKGROUND/HISTORY: 
 
The subject parcel was created, legally, by an Assessor’s Office Split in April of 2023.   Though not 
required, all resulting parcels of this split met the requirements of Planning and Development Services’ 
Plat Alteration process. 
 
At the Thursday, August 17 th, 2023 Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission Hearing, the 
Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended denial of this project.  
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The proposed project is a  80’ monopole wireless communication tower and its associated facilities.  
This tower will be located at least 160’ away from all property lines on APN # 304 -36-003H.  The 
tower, and all associated equipment, will be located in a 40’ x 40’ leased area, surrounded by a 8’ tall 
chain link fence with three strand barb wire atop the fence. 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 11 – (R1-10) SINGLE FAMILY RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 10,000  
      SQUARE FEET PER DWELLING UNIT 
 
Section 1101 – Purpose.  
 
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to conserve and protect single-family residential 
development where minimum lots of not less than ten-thousand (10,000) square feet in area are 
suitable and appropriate taking into consideration existing con ditions, including present use of 
land, present lot sizes, future land use needs, and the availability of public utilities.  Principal 
uses permitted in this zoning district include single-family dwellings, churches, schools, parks, 
playgrounds, and other community facilities. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
  
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts 
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further 
prohibited by this Article. 
 
34. Television, wireless towers, and radio transmitter towers and stations, subject to 
approval by Federal Regulatory Agencies. 
 
Section 2521 – Wireless Communication Facilities 
 
The purpose of this Section is to establish rules and regulations for the siting of wireless 
communications facilities.  The goals of this Section are to provide for the development of

<!-- page 364 -->
Board of Supervisors              3 | P a g e  
 
wireless communication services County-wide by: 
 
1. Considering the public health, safety, and welfare. 
 
2. Considering impact on adjacent properties. 
 
3. Encouraging configuration which minimizes visual impact through proper siting, design, 
landscape, and camouflage techniques. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
Comprehensive Plan 
 
Land Use Element 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging development. 
 
 Policy 6 -B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses 
 including, but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
▪ The applicant has indicated that they will set back their wireless communication 
tower a minimum of 160’ from all property lines, which meets the increased 
setback requirements of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance for facilities of 
this nature that fall under the residential property designation.   
 
 Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9-F: Encourage development/installation of technology improvements including, 
but not limited to, high speed Internet lines and other communications improvements in 
Navajo County. 
 
▪ The applicant is proposing the addition of this wireless communication tower and 
associated facilities in order to provide higher quality wireless service for 
residents, businesses, visitors, and emergency services in this region of Navajo 
County. 
 
Analysis: 
 
This project meets all relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan.  The 
Comprehensive Plan encourages buffers that include greater setbacks, which this project provides via  
setbacks that are two feet horizontally for every one vertical foot of height of the tower.  The 
Comprehensive Plan also encourages improvements to communications within the County, which the 
applicant has indicated this project is aiming to provide. 
 
This project, and its submittal, meet all of the requirements of Section 2521 of the Navajo County 
Zoning Ordinance, which regulates wireless communication facilities.  
 
As of August 2nd, 2023, Planning and Development Services Staff have not received any comments in

Board of Supervisors              4 | P a g e  
 
favor of, nor against, the proposed project.

PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION/STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended denial of this Special Use 
Permit. 
 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit,  
subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) monopole wireless communication tower, not to 
exceed 80 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for the operation of the tower on 
the subject property. 
 
2. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower and facilities 
prior to commencing construction. 
 
3. The applicant shall meet all State and Federal requirements concerning communication towers 
prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
4. The tower shall be constructed as a monopole, as indicated in the supplied plans.  The owner of 
the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
5. A fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director shall be constructed 
to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
6. A Geotechnical Report for the Access Easement shall be submitted and approved. 
 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any 
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this permit. 
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from the date of 
Board approval, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo County 
Planning and Development Services that the tower is no longer being utilized. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner and/or applicant shall cause, at their 
expense, the removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users who may be 
interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon commercially 
reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not technically feasible. 
 
12. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific use and 
to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final inspection by the Planning

<!-- page 367 -->
Board of Supervisors              6 | P a g e  
 
and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, signifying that all 
zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, 
the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be transferable an d shall run with the land, whereupon the 
maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other 
provisions of this title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning and 
Development Staff on the fifth (5 th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and every five (5) 
years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in compliance with all conditions 
contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of compliance with these conditions, this Special 
Use Permit and its privileges may be revoked.

9/15/2023 
 
ASACSafety.com 
 
1 
 
 
Pinnacle Consulting 
 
 This site known as AZ11 153 Rooster, Bourdon Ranch Road was 
evaluated by Airspace Safety Analysis and Compliance (ASAC). 
 
 ASAC has been in the business of evaluating obstructions to air 
navigation for over 30 years.  We have worked with the FAA under contract, 
the DOD, and private industry over the last 30 years. 
 
 This site was evaluated by the manager of the obstruction evaluation 
group within the FAA and was issued a determination of no hazard.   
 
 The FAA is the controlling authority of all airspace over the United 
States by decree from Congress.  No other entity is allowed to restrict the use 
of the airspace.  Municipalities are allowed to restrict what can be 
accomplished on the ground with restrictive zoning, but not the airspace.  
These zoning restrictions may affect other property owners right to use their 
property and usually take considerable legal planning and zoning actions. 
 
 Our evaluation shows that the FAA does not protect the airport known 
as White Mountain Lake.  The concern is that there is a helicopter operation to 
the east, where this proposed antenna tower of 80’ would be a safety issue.  
We could not locate any published routes either inbound or outbound.  The 
data that was last submitted to the FAA (in 1980), showed that there are no 
aircraft/helicopters stationed at the field.  We noted two on the tarmac while 
doing this review. 
 
 Our evaluation agrees with the FAA that there are no known air safety 
issues.  If there were a helicopter issue to the east, the rapid rising terrain, up 
to 200’ higher than the tower site, would be more of a concern.  Helicopters 
are directed by the Federal Aviation Rules (FAR) to operate at or above 300’ 
over the ground or tallest structure.  The terrain in the area will dictate that a 
helicopter would be 400’ over the tower site.  There is also a 60’ to 70’ 
powerline on the east side of the dirt access road in this direction. 
 
 Our research did not show any noise abatement areas. On the contrary, 
we show that there are notices that there are military aircraft operations in 
vicinity of the airport and the airport is listed as a use at own risk with wildlife 
and livestock in vicinity of runway.

<!-- page 404 -->
9/15/2023 
 
ASACSafety.com 
 
 The FCC also protects the construction of personal wireless service 
facilities under Section 704(a) of the 1996 Communications Act.   
 
Federal law allows cities to deny construction permit applications for 
telecommunications towers. The denial, however, must be based on a 
reasoned approach, otherwise the FCC is authorized to pre-empt the local 
decision and grant the construction permit.  This denial must be in writing. 
 
Without adopting a telecommunications tower policy, it is doubtful that 
a municipality’s denial of a construction permit will be seen as resulting from 
a reasoned approach.  
 
 
 
Dale Smith, CEO 
ASAC

1. REVIEW OF AGENDA BY COMMISSIONERS: 
 
Nothing was discussed. 
 
2. CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA: Individuals may address the Commission on any 
relevant issue for up to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Commission members may not respond 
to any comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be placed on a 
future agenda. 
 
Nothing was discussed. 
 
3. CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISSION HEARING OF: July 20, 2023. 
 
Vice-Chair Smith motioned to accept the minutes. Commissioner Muder seconded the motion. The motion 
passed unanimously, 8 - 0. (Commissioner Lister was not present for this portion of the hearing and joined 
later) 
 
4. SUP 23-009 AZ11-153 ROOSTER WIRELESS COMMUNICATION FACILITY, DISTRICT III: Consideration of a request by 
Pinnacle Consulting for a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of an 80’ monopole wireless communication 
tower and associated facilities in the White Mountain Lakes area. APN#: 304-36-003H, T11N, R22E, S13 of the GSRM. 
Owner: Richard C. and Pauline G. Brooksby. Agent: Pinnacle Consulting. 
 
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission. Mr. Cooper showed the Commission an 
overview of the area and the various landmarks and features within the vicinity of the proposed tower. The project was 
located within the White Mountain Lakes area just off Bourdon Ranch Road. Mr. Cooper stated that the Applicant 
proposed an 80-foot Monopole Wireless Communications tower and associated facilities. Mr. Cooper shared with the 
Commission a site plan of the project, as well as the side views of the proposed tower. Mr. Cooper also showed the 
Commission some simulated visuals of what the tower would look like once erected. 
 
Mr. Cooper then went over the Applicant's community outreach efforts. The Applicants hosted a public meeting on June 
7th, 2023 and Mr. Cooper provided the Applicant's summary of questions and concerns that had been brought up by the

<!-- page 410 -->
2 of 5  
public, along with a list of attendees. 
 
Mr. Cooper then shared with the Commission a Determination of No Hazard to Air Navigation received late that morning from 
the FAA. Mr. Cooper stated that the document would be included in the Board of Supervisors packets and provided paper copies 
to the Commission as Staff had received it just that morning, long after uploading the packet for the Commission. 
 
Mr. Cooper then went over Staff’s recommended conditions, if the project was approved. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked Staff to clarify the stipulation regarding construction time. Mr. Cooper explained that the 
Applicant would have to start construction within 12 months, or the Special Use Permit’s privileges (SUP) would lapse. 
 
Chair Latham asked Staff to explain the removal process for the components and tower if not being utilized. Mr. Cooper 
explained that once a component or the tower was no longer in use the component would be removed, or in the event of the 
tower no longer being in use, the entire facility would be removed. 
 
With no further questions, Chair Latham opened the Public Comment period. There were 
no comments in favor of the project. 
There were several members of the public that spoke against the project, including: 
Steven-Charles Brown, Anthony Martinez, Tyler Legg, Buz Essel, Tony Tangelos, Dave Smith, Robert Meyer 
 
Concerns from the public Included: 
If the Geotechnical Report was the same as an Environmental Review and if applicant had gone through 
proper procedures. 
Lack of Access to the Application. 
Potential Health issues due to the 5G that could impact wildlife and 
children. Property Values decreasing. 
If the tower was even necessary, as there was existing coverage for the 
area. 5G causing Radar Altimeter issues with aircraft. 
The tower being in the landing flight path from the East approach, adding that changing the flight path would 
cause an annoyance to the neighborhood. 
The proposed tower being too close to the airport and helipads. 
 
Chair Latham asked Staff to address the concerns of the Public. Mr. 
Cooper addressed the concerns in turn: 
 
A Geotechnical Report has to do with studying the soil composition, to see if it would be able to support the 
weight of the tower and a fully loaded fire truck in case of a fire. These are not the same as an Impact Study, but 
that would come as part of the NEPA process with the Federal Regulations that the Applicant would have to 
abide by, as stated in the conditions of the SUP. 
To Address the comment that the application was not accessible, the documents submitted were available 
online and could be accessed through the website’s agenda software.  The only case where a document is not 
uploaded for public view is when it is submitted late, as was the case with materials supplied by the Public and 
the FAA letter. Mr. Cooper further detailed where to find the archives used by Navajo County, as well as how to 
contact Staff if there was an issue with the uploaded documents. 
 
As for the possible health hazards, Staff has not received any peer-reviewed viable evidence that proved that 
5G was harmful to health or wildlife. Mr. Cooper stated that the Applicant had a Radio Frequency Engineer 
available to speak to the Commission about the health and safety aspects of RF waves. 
 
As far as the information regarding the property value, that was outside of Staff purview, and would be up to 
the Board of Supervisors to consider. 
To address the necessity for the tower, Applicants are required to provide proof of need, and the applicants 
had submitted an RF study and had an RF Engineer online to address any questions the Commission may 
have. If there was not a proven need for the tower, it would be within the purview of the Commission to 
recommend denial, and Board of Supervisors to deny the SUP, per the Zoning Ordinance. 
As for how 5G could affect Radar Altimeters, Staff was aware of the statement made by the FAA saying that 
there was a possibility that some models of altimeters could be affected but noted that there was guidance for 
adjusting the equipment to make up for the possible interference with extra shielding or by adjusting the 
settings on the equipment per FAA standards, and the FAA had listed contacts for reporting Altimeter issues. 
 
As far as the Flight Path being affected, Staff has the document from the FAA stating that the tower would not 
cause issues, and Staff was deferring to Federal recommendation. 
Chair Latham addressed the Public, stating that the FAA letter noted that the physical tower was in an acceptable location 
and did not address 5G.

<!-- page 411 -->
3 of 5  
Commissioner Rogers asked if the Applicant was available to speak. Mr. Cooper confirmed that he was. 
 
Vice-Chair Smith stated that she wished for an unbiased study as a resource to use as a standard, noting how 
misinformation was an issue. 
 
Mr. Cooper stated that the recently passed Cell Tower Ordinance Amendment had been researched by an outside source that 
had not indicated any concerns over new technologies. Mr. Cooper also noted that Staff has not found, nor received, any peer-
reviewed studies that proved 5G caused undue harm or health concerns. 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked Staff how many 5G towers were already in the County. Mr. Cooper replied that 
unfortunately Staff does not have an estimate as some towers were outside of the County’s jurisdiction, within the 
Municipalities, or on Forest Service land and many towers within Navajo County’s jurisdiction had been updated. 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked if there was a stipulation the Commission could add that if 5G was found to cause undue health 
effects that the tower would be removed. Mr. Cooper responded that, if the Commission wished to add a recommendation, it was 
within the Commission’s ability, after consulting with Legal. 
 
Chair Latham noted that while he knew it was not easy to trust the authorities, sometimes it was necessary to have faith 
that the Public’s best interests were at heart. 
 
Commissioner Howe then asked about the proximity of the tower to the helipads and questioned if the Applicant had 
considered other locations further away from the airport. 
 
Mr. John Katsiris, the Applicant, stated that other locations had been explored within the needed range but stated that the current 
property owner was the only one that had agreed to lease the land for the tower. Mr. Katsiris further stated that the project had 
already been registered with the FAA and FCC, and the other ideal locations were owned by the Forest Service from which it was 
difficult to lease land. 
 
Mr. Katsiris also stated that their Engineer was available to speak to the Commission to explain how the 5G radio 
frequency worked. 
 
Mr. Steven Kennedy gave a detailed explanation of the differences between harmful ionizing radio waves and 
non-ionizing radio waves. 5G used non-ionizing radio waves, and the side effects would be generating heat. Mr. Kennedy further 
detailed how the Federal standards dictated the accepted levels of energy, and how their research across several different 
agencies, including the FCC, OSHA, and several people from the medical community determined a set limit for the absorption rate 
of energy or heat from these transmitters. Mr. Kennedy stated that the acceptable limit was 1.4 watts per centimeter squared, 
over a period of 30 minutes. Mr. Kennedy then explained that this measurement was what was considered safe for a working 
environment, such as rooftop access to the transmitters, and half of this measurement was what was considered safe for the 
General Public, noting that holding a cellphone would expose you to half of the limit for the General Public. Mr. Kennedy began to 
explain what the level of absorption rate would be for the General Public around the tower, explaining that at ground level it 
would only be 1/10th of the safe limit for the General Public. 
 
Mr. Kennedy had to be cut short on his presentation for time constraints and experienced some technical issues before he could 
detail any further and rejoined after the discussion continued. 
 
Commissioner Rogers noted that there was not enough viable information to make decisions based on potential health 
concerns but did wish to find out more about the positioning of the tower in relation to the airport. 
 
Commissioner Rogers then asked the Chair for Mr. Anthony Martinez, an Air Medical Team Supervisor from Air Evac that 
came to speak in opposition of the project, to address the Commission for more information. Chair Latham allowed this 
request. 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked if the fact that the neighbors complained about the noise and forced the pilots to only be able to 
approach and land in certain areas had anything to do with why this tower was an issue for the pilots, and if other options for 
landing had been considered. 
 
Mr. Martinez stated that the weather patterns around the area made it very difficult to navigate the airspace and still comply 
with noise abatement, noting that the pilots would receive several complaints if there were any deviations from the approved 
flight paths. 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked if the abatement was FAA enforced or if it was only accommodating the neighborhood. Mr. 
Martinez deferred to Mr. Tangelos to answer this question. 
 
Mr. Tony Tangelos, an owner of the Airpark, confirmed that they were accommodating neighbors. Mr. Tangelos explained that 
there were two reasons the helicopter pilots approached from the east. The FAA’s regulations required aircraft to fly a certain 
height above residences and given how close the homes were to the runway, it posed a challenge to meet the noise abatement 
standards set by the FAA. This alone was a challenge, but the second reason for approaching from the East was due to the wind 
that would come from the South-West and blow North-East, as helicopters had to approach to land flying into the wind and the 
tower was unfortunately in their path. 
 
Commissioner Booher asked about the average number of flights per day. Mr. Martinez stated that the average was

<!-- page 412 -->
4 of 5  
about 1 flight per day. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked what the average height approaching the landing pads would be, citing Bourdon Ranch 
Road being between the airpark and the tower. 
 
Mr. Tyler Legg, a helicopter pilot for Air Evac that worked with Mr. Martinez approached to answer Commissioner 
Muder’s question, stating the helicopter would be at about 150 feet above ground level. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked if fixed-wing aircraft would be approaching from that direction. Mr. Legg said that planes 
would have to align with the runway and would not approach from the east, whereas helicopters went straight for the 
helipads. 
 
Commissioner Booher asked if a quarter of a mile was not enough space to navigate around the tower. 
 
Mr. Legg said that it was possible to maneuver around the tower, but that it would be dangerous to navigate in the dark, 
and that depth perception at night would make this an especially difficult obstacle. 
 
Commissioner Booher asked what a preferred distance for the tower would be, to which Mr. Legg said half a mile to 
a mile. 
 
Commissioner Rogers asked if the proposal was, in his opinion, a safety hazard to pilots. Mr. Legg said that it was 
indeed a hazard, specifically to helicopter pilots, especially at night. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked if Mr. Legg had experienced 5G interference. Mr. Legg stated he had several instances 
with a particular helicopter in Phoenix where 5G was much more present. He further stated that it would not cause the 
altimeters to go out, but that the altimeter would give false readings or act erratically. 
 
Commissioner Booher asked what the FAA required regarding dealing with this issue and if there was a workaround. 
Mr. Legg said their guidance was to simply turn their cellphones’ airplane mode on while flying, know how high your 
landing elevation is, adjust the altimeter settings, being able to look outside and see where the ground is. Mr. Legg said 
that the tower would be above the level of the airport, and that could cause issues with judging distance, especially at 
night when visibility was low, and pilots were not able to see to properly judge distance. 
 
One of the Applicant’s representatives, Mr. Graham Chapman, requested to address these concerns. Chair Latham 
permitted the Applicant to speak. 
 
Mr. Chapman deferred to Mr. Kennedy, the RF Engineer who gave a brief history of the CBAND interference, stating 
that the various carriers had delayed the release of 5G to ensure the FAA had proper procedures for dealing with 
altimeter issues. Mr. Kennedy further went on to say that the equipment that was affected by the interference had a 
defect that make it unable to filter some frequencies, and the issue was easily corrected by adding in shielding, or by 
replacing the altimeters. Mr. Kennedy noted that it was a mandate by the FAA that all aircraft update their shielding or 
replaced their altimeters by March of 2024. 
 
With this, Chair Latham closed the public comments. 
 
Commissioner Howe stated that he had some significant concerns about the proposed location, given that the 
prevailing winds would force the helicopters to approach from the east. Chair Latham agreed. 
 
With there being no further discussion, Commissioner Rogers motioned to recommend Denial of the Special Use 
Permit to the Board of Supervisors.  Commissioner Booher Seconded the motion. 
 
Motion passed unanimously, 9 - 0. 
 
5. SUP 23-008 AZ11-172 STALLION WIRELESS COMMUNICATION TOWER, DISTRICT IV: Consideration of a 
request by Pinnacle Consulting for a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 100' monoelm 
wireless communication tower and associated facilities in the Linden area. APN#: 409-22-005C, T10N, R21E, 
S06 of the GSRM. Owner: Ralph E. and Julie A. Forney. Agent: Pinnacle Consulting. 
 
Mr. Tyler Richards, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. Mr. Richards stated that this was a wireless 
communications tower in the Linden area, noting the location of the tower in relation to various landmarks and the 
road. Mr. Richards also shared the site plan, stating that the lease area was 40 by 40 feet, and that as this was not in a 
platted subdivision, the applicants were able to go above the residential limit of 80 feet and were requesting a 100-foot 
5G tower with monoelm stealthing, sharing a side-view of the proposed stealthing. 
 
Mr. Richards shared the simulations provided by the Applicant, and the Applicant’s Public Outreach efforts, stating that 
no one came to their public meeting, and thus did not have any notes to address.  Staff received one comment in

<!-- page 413 -->
5 of 5  
opposition regarding the project, stating concern over the beautification of the area, and some health concerns. Mr. 
Richards then stated that Staff recommended approval of the Special Use Permit, with conditions proposed by Staff. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked about the condition that the site be inspected every 5 years, to which Mr. Richards 
replied that this condition had been given to previous projects by the Board of Supervisors and would now be added 
to all subsequent projects. 
 
Commissioner Muder asked Staff if building permits were all that were required for building these towers. Mr. Cody 
Cooper, Planning Manager, responded that the Applicants would still need to complete Federal licensing. 
 
The Applicant, Mr. Graham Chapman from Pinnacle Consulting, then introduced the project to the Commission, stating 
that this project would provide a great deal of difference in the area’s service that would benefit the community. Mr. 
Chapman further stated that the Elm tree stealthing was chosen as it was their highest quality stealthing and looked best. 
 
Chair Latham opened the Public Comment period. 
 
With no public comments, Chair Latham closed the Public Comment period. 
 
Commissioner Howe had concerns with the stealthing, noting that using a deciduous tree design that was 
supposed to lose its leaves in the winter being green year-round would look out of place, but that was the only concern 
he had regarding the project. 
 
With no further comments, Commissioner Booher motioned to recommend approval of the Special Use Permit 
to the Board of Supervisors. Commissioner Muder seconded the motion. 
 
The motion passed unanimously, 9 - 0.

FCC 854R 
 
You must comply with all applicable FCC obstruction marking and lighting requirements, as set forth  in Part 17 of the 
Commission’s Rules (47 C.F.R. Part 17).  These rules include, but are not limited to:  
• Posting the Registration Number:  The Antenna Structure Registration Number must be displayed in a
conspicuous place so that it is readily visible near the base of the antenna structure.  Materials used to
display the Registration Number must be weather-resistant and of sufficient size to be easily seen at the
base of the antenna structure.  Exceptions exist for certain historic structures.  See 47 C.F. R. 17.4(g)-(h).
• Inspecting lights and equipment:  The obstruction lighting must be observed at least every 24 hours in
order to detect any outages or malfunctions.  Lighting equipment, indicators, and associated devices must
be inspected at least once every three months.
• Reporting outages and malfunctions:  When any top steady-burning light or a flashing light (in any
position) burns out or malfunctions, the outage must be reported to the nearest FAA Flight Service Station,
unless corrected within 30 minutes.  The FAA must again be notified when the light is restored.  The owner
must also maintain a log of these outages and malfunctions.
• Maintaining assigned painting:  The antenna structure must be repainted as often as necessary to
maintain good visibility.
• Complying with environmental rules:  If you certified that grant of this registration would not have a
significant environmental impact, you must nevertheless maintain all pertinent records and be ready to
provide documentation supporting this certification and compliance with the rules, in the event that such
information is requested by the Commission pursuant to 47 C.F.R. 1.1307(d).
• Updating information:  The owner must notify the FCC of proposed modifications to this structure; of any
change in ownership; or, within 30 days of dismantlement of the structure.
Copies of the Code of Federal Regulations (which contain the FCC’s antenna structure registration rules, 47 C.F.R Part 
17) are available from the Government Printing Office (GPO).  To purchase CFR volumes, call (202) 512-1800.  For
GPO Customer Service, call (202) 512-1803.  For additional FCC information, consult the Antenna Homepage on the 
internet at https://www.fcc.gov/antenna-structure-registration or call (877) 480-3201 (TTY 717-338-2824).

Case Description for ASN 2023-AWP-10402-OE

Part 77 Obstruction Standard(s) Exceeded and Aeronautical Impacts, if any:
Aeronautical study revealed that the temporary structure will not exceed any Part 77 obstruction standard.
Aeronautical study confirmed that the temporary structure will have no effect on any existing or proposed
arrival, departure or en route instrument/visual flight rules (IFR/VFR) operations or procedures. Additionally,
aeronautical study confirmed that the temporary structure will have no physical or electromagnetic effect on the
operation of air navigation and communications facilities and will not impact any airspace and routes used by
the military. Based on this aeronautical study, the FAA finds that the temporary structure will have no adverse
effect on air navigation and will not impact any aeronautical operations or procedures.
Based on this aeronautical study, the structure would not constitute a substantial adverse effect on aeronautical
operations or procedures because it will be temporary. The temporary structure would not be considered a
hazard to air navigation provided all of the conditions specified in this determination are strictly met.
Based on this evaluation, marking and lighting are not necessary for aviation safety. However, if marking/
lighting are accomplished on a voluntary basis, we recommend it be installed in accordance with FAA Advisory
circular 70/7460-1 M.
This determination expires on 01/12/2025 unless extended, revised, or terminated by the issuing office.
It is required that FAA Form 7460-2, Notice of Actual Construction or Alteration, be e-filed within 5 days after
the temporary structure is dismantled.
NOTE: REQUEST FOR EXTENSION OF THE EFFECTIVE PERIOD OF THIS DETERMINATION MUST
BE E-FILED AT LEAST 15 DAYS PRIOR TO THE EXPIRATION DATE. AFTER RE-EVALUATION
OF CURRENT OPERATIONS IN THE AREA OF THE STRUCTURE TO DETERMINE THAT NO
SIGNIFICANT AERONAUTICAL CHANGES HAVE OCCURRED, YOUR DETERMINATION MAY BE
ELIGIBLE FOR ONE EXTENSION OF THE EFFECTIVE PERIOD.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. c. 2.        
Meeting Date: 09/26/2023  
Title: SUP 23-008 AZ11-172 STALLION WIRELESS COMMUNICATION
FACILITY, DISTRICT IV
Submitted By: Tyler Richards, Planner I
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Development
Presented By: Tyler Richards
Motion before the Board:
PUBLIC HEARING: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution 36-2023,
approving/denying a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction of a 100' mono-elm
wireless communication tower and associated facilities in the Linden Area. APN# 409-22-005C.
Background:
Currently, County records indicate an existing residence on the property, as well as a
commercial brewery on the west-side of the property. This proposal would expand the usage of
the site to include a wireless communication tower and its associated facilities, which would be
an appropriate usage within the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, if approved for a Special
Use Permit.
On August 17th, 2023, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously
recommended approval of the Special Use Permit for AZ11-172 Stallion Wireless
Communication Facility. 
 
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended approval of
the Special Use Permit at the August 17th, 2023, Planning and Zoning Commission hearing,
subject to the conditions as recommended by the Navajo County Planning and Development
Services Staff in the Staff Report.

PROJECT NARRATIVE 
 
PROJECT NAME: AZ11-172 Stallion / AZ3 Shamama 
 
APPLICANT: Sun State Towers, on behalf of Verizon Wireless 
 
REQUEST: Approval of Special Use Permit 
For a New Wireless Communication Facility 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Graham Chapman 
Site Acquisition Specialist  
1426 N Marvin St, Suite 101 
Gilbert, AZ 85233 
M: (650) 815-5267 
O: (480) 664-9588 ext. 255 
F: (480) 664-9850 
E: graham.chapman@pinnacleco.net

<!-- page 435 -->
Reasons for the Proposed Special Use Permit & Benefits to the Area 
 
We are proposing to place a stealthed wireless telecommunication facility at parcel 409-22-005C 
in Show Low because we want to provide better cellular coverage and capacity to mobile users 
in the surrounding area.  
 
Currently, there is a severe lack of service in the rural neighborhood around the proposed site, 
and mobile phones are reporting low-quality connections for both indoor and outdoor service. 
This is critical to fix, because 96% of Americans own a cellular phone and 57% of American 
homes rely exclusively on cellular phones.  
 
In addition, there is a lack of service along Highway 260, which is only a quarter mile south of 
the proposed site. This is a big issue, not only for the residents in this area, but also for all 
commuters along Hwy 260 as well as emergency service responders like fire and police who 
need people to have a better signal in those areas, as 84% or more of all 911 emergency calls are 
made from wireless devices.   
 
If we place our facility at the requested parcel, we can fill in a huge gap of cellular coverage and 
capacity that the community has been dealing with for a long time.  
 
On the following page are coverage maps prepared by a registered radio-frequency engineer that 
show the current coverage in the area and the proposed increased coverage if we are able to place 
our facility.

<!-- page 437 -->
Explanation of Proposed Use 
 
Our proposal is to place a facility in the empty farmland area of 1058 Burton Road, Show Low, 
AZ 85901 in a 40’ x 40’ ground-space lease area. We also propose to add camouflaging 
components to the tower to make it appear like an elm tree, which will blend in well with the 
presence of other trees and foliage in the area.  
 
Our exact scope of work is as follows: 
 
- Install 100’ Sun State Towers Monoelm 
- Install 40’x40’x8’ chain-link fence with 3-strand barb wire 
- Install 12’ wide double swing access gate 
- Install 12 antennas 
- Install 12 radio-heads 
- Install 1 OVP-12 
- Install antenna mounting frame 
- Install 2 hybriflex cables 
- Install 3  4"Ø underground conduits 
- Install 4’x12’ concrete pad 
- Install RF cabinet 
- Install power cabinet 
- Install 4’8’ concrete pad 
- Install 50kW Diesel back-up generator 
- Install GPS antenna 
- Install 2 H-Frames 
- Install electrical Service 
- Install telco service 
 
On the following page are images from our site plan that illustrate and explain our proposed use 
on a more technical and visual level.

<!-- page 439 -->
Schedule of Development 
 
 
While different projects and different scopes may require slightly altered construction schedules, 
above is a general development schedule that all Sun State Tower projects generally follow.  
 
Once zoning submittal is achieved, we will follow the Special Use Permit process until the SUP 
is granted. Once that is granted, we move onto building permit submittal. Once that building 
permit is granted, we then move onto actual construction.  
 
Actual construction will generally follow the activity table above. Typically, construction of the 
Sun State Tower will take approximately one month, and then construction and installation of the 
Verizon equipment on the tower takes another month. So in total, our tower construction time 
from start to finish is about 2 months.

RE: Special Use Permit Application for Wireless Facility Project for “AZ11-172 Stallion / AZ3 Shamama”  
 
Dear Navajo County Planning & Development Services Department, 
 
Per your new code regarding Wireless Communication Facilities, special use permit applicants shall submit as 
part of the zoning submittal, “Certification that the wireless communication facility, as represented in the 
application, will comply with all FAA, FCC and other applicable regulations”. 
 
Due to prolonged lead times to obtain environmental services that produce the FAA/FCC approval (roughly 60-
120 days), we cannot produce that certification as of today, and we are asking for relief from this submittal item 
for the initial submittal. Please note that Sun State Towers has submitted for FAA & FCC review and 
determination of approval via its consultant Trileaf. We expect to have their respective approvals in 60-120 
days, and we will relay to Navajo County upon receipt and/or prior to hearing schedule. We also follow 
FAA/FCC rules and regulations when engineering our projects, and below is an additional TOWAIR 
determination results that illustrate that the structure will not require registration.  
 
 
Best regards, 
 
________________________________________ 
Graham Chapman, Site Acquisition Specialist 
480-664-9588 ext. 255

<!-- page 441 -->
Compatibility of Proposed Use with Adjacent Uses and Character of the Area 
 
Our proposed parcel (409-22-005C) is currently a mostly empty lot with a home at the very 
north-west corner of the property, and the property is zoned as “A-General” but assessed as 
residential. Many of the surrounding properties in the vicinity are also all zoned as A-General but 
serve and are assessed as homes with large square footage and large open spaces.  
 
The purpose of A-General zoning is “to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County . . . 
and . . . Uses permitted in the "A-General Zone", include farm and non-farm residential uses, 
farms, recreational, institutional, commercial and industrial uses as specifically listed in this 
Article.  Other uses may be permitted as Special Uses under Article 20.” 
 
While our use will require a Special Use Permit, it is going to be a commercial use that would be 
allowed in the A-General zoning designation. It is also a use that will benefit all the other 
allowable uses in that area (farm, non-farm, recreational, institutional, commercial, etc.) because 
wireless capacity helps all of those uses in different ways. Furthermore, stealthing it as a natural 
tree will further maintain the rural, woody character of the area.

RE: Special Use Permit Application for Wireless Facility Project for “AZ11-172 Stallion / AZ3 Shamama”  
 
Dear Navajo County Planning & Development Services Department, 
 
Per your new code regarding Wireless Communication Facilities, special use permit applicants shall submit as 
part of the zoning submittal, “Certification of whether the applicant is applying for collocation treatment, and 
how many carriers could be accommodated on the facility with adequate signal coverage”. 
 
 
 
This document will serve as our confirmation that this Sun State Towers facility will not only accommodate 
Verizon Wireless equipment as the anchor carrier at a 96’, but will also accommodate co-location requests from 
at least two (2) future carriers at centerlines of 86’ and 76’, as referenced in the diagram above.  
 
 
 
Best regards, 
 
________________________________________

RE: Neighborhood meeting for new proposed Wireless Facility (AZ11-172 Stallion / AZ3 Shamama)  
       Near the cross streets of Linger Lane  and  Burton Road 
       Coordinates: Latitude 34.2891483°, Longitude -110.1563275°  
 
Dear Neighbor,   
 
You are receiving this letter because according to the Navajo County Assessors records, you are a 
property owner located in the area surrounding a property that will be home to a new Wireless 
Communication Facility.  
 
This proposed facility is needed to help fill a gap in wireless coverage in the area and will allow for 
increased access to advanced wireless voice and data service.  
 
Pinnacle Consulting, Inc. will hold a neighborhood meeting online via Zoom on Thursday, May 11th
 at 
6pm to 7pm, for the purpose of providing information concerning the proposed construction of a new 
Wireless Communication Facility.  
 
Join Zoom Meeting 
https://zoom.us/j/99408731102?pwd=WXBOdlZ2UVpyM3JVSjhjeGhCbkpyUT09  
 
Meeting ID: 994 0873 1102 
Passcode: 788901 
 
At the meeting I will discuss the proposed Wireless Communication Facility, as well as show photo 
simulations of the proposed tower, and will be available to answer any questions you may have.  
 
If you have questions concerning this matter but are unable to attend or would like a direct link invite to 
the zoom meeting, please contact Graham Chapman, Site Acquisition Specialist at 
graham.chapman@pinnacleco.net or 480-664-9588 ext. 255.  
 
Sincerely,

Health & Safety of Cellular T owers
The Federal Communications Commission (FCC) has information about the 
safety of RF Emissions: 
http://www.fcc.gov/oet/rfsafety/rf-faqs.html
The American Cancer Society (ACS) has conducted several studies you can 
find them here:  
http://www.cancer.org/cancer/cancercauses/othercarcinogens/athome/cellul
ar-phone-towers

<!-- page 523 -->
Federal T elecommunications Act of 1996
This is a federal law requiring jurisdictions to authorize requests for 
wireless facilities in a “reasonable period of time”
The jurisdiction (Navajo County) has 150-days to process our application 
once submitted
If they fail to act within that time-frame, we can file suit for “failure to act” 
under section 332(c)(7)(B)(v) within 30-days after that limit has been 
reached

Questions/Comments/Concerns?

concerns to Graham Chapman at graham.chapman@pinnacleco.net

May 22nd, 2023 
  
RE: Proposed Wireless Telecommunications Facility (Project Name: AZ11-172 Stallion / AZ3 Shamama)  
       Near the cross streets of Linger Lane  and  Burton Road 
       Latitude 34.2891483°, Longitude -110.1563275°  
 
Hello Timber Mesa Fire District,   
 
You are receiving this letter because, according to the Navajo County GIS maps, you are fire department 
within 5 miles of a property that will be home to a new Wireless Communication Facility.  
 
Sun State Towers proposes to place a 100’ camouflaged wireless communication tower at parcel 409-22-
005C (1058 Burton Road, Show Low, AZ 85901) that will help fill a gap in wireless coverage and 
capacity in the area and will allow for increased access to advanced wireless voice and data service..  
 
Sun State Towers will be pursuing a Special Use Permit to place the facility there, and per Navajo 
County’s new zoning ordinance regulations governing applications for wireless communication facilities, 
Navajo County would like us to provide: 
 
“Certification, or other appropriate documentation, that policing, fire departments, public safety, 
water, and local governments having jurisdiction within five (5) miles of the site have been 
notified of the application” 
 
 
 
You are the only police, fire, public safety, water, or local department within 5 mi of the site. If you have 
questions concerning this facility, please contact me at the contact information below. 
 
Sincerely,  
 
Graham Chapman  
Site Acquisition Specialist at Pinnacle Consulting, Inc.  
1426 N Marvin St, Suite 101  
Gilbert, AZ 85233  
O: (480) 664-9588 ext. 255  
E: graham.chapman@pinnacleco.net

requires us to   alert all police, ﬁre departments, public safety, water, and local governments having

<!-- page 530 -->
Certification of No Adequate Existing Facilities Within 5 miles 
Per the new language of Navajo County Zoning Ordinance TXT-A 22-003, Amendment to 
Article 25 – Establishment Of Rules And Regulations For The Siting Of Wireless 
Communications Facilities, one of the requirements for a Special Use Permit application 
submittal reads as follows: 
 
“Certification that no Navajo County or municipal owned site, or existing wireless 
facility reasonably meets the needs of the applicant, listing all such sites within five (5) 
miles of the proposed site and the reason each is not physically adequate for reasonable 
commercial coverage, or not economically feasible for location” 
 
 
The image above is a search ring of all wireless facility sites within a 5-mile radius of our 
proposed location. As you can see, there is only listed search ring; it is for the American Towers 
project “AZ3 Show High”, and it has yet to be built. The only other closest facility is outside of 
that 5-mile search, and it’s a rooftop-facility that is only 14’ high, which would not adequately 
address the needs of the community we are proposing to place our tower in.

Subject:     Engineering Narrative Letter 
 
Tower Owner:      Sun State Towers 
      Site Name:       Stallion 
Site Number:           AZ11
‐172 
 
Site Data:      1058 Burton Road, Show Low, Navajo County, AZ 85901 
      34° 17' 20.934” N, 110° 09' 22.779" W 
      ± 100’ Proposed Monoelm Tower 
 
Dear William Bess,  
 
The purpose of this letter is to provide a written engineering narrative confirming the structural stability 
of the proposed monoelm tower, 
foundation and antenna mounting attachments at the subject site. 
 
The proposed monoelm tower, foundation and antenna mounts were designed in accordance with the 
2018  International  Building  Code  with  Navajo  County  Amendments,  ASCE  7‐16  and  the  adopted  by 
reference standard ANSI/TIA‐222‐H, Structural Standard for Antenna Supporting Structures and Antennas.
  
The following structural design criteria was used for the proposed tower, foundation and antenna mount 
design: 
 Wind Speed: 115 mph (Ultimate) 
 Service Wind Speed: 60 mph (Ultimate – Deflection limits only) 
 Risk Category II 
 Exposure Category C 
 Topographic Category 1 
 Ice: 0.25” w/ 30 mph wind gust speed (Ignored per TIA‐222‐H 2.6.4) 
 Seismic Design Category B (Ss = 0.258 and S1 = 0.080) 
The proposed 100’ monoelm tower and foundation were designed to support carrier antenna arrays at 
the 96’, 86’ and 76’ elevations.  The 96’ elevation will be for Verizon and the loading will include (12) panel 
antennas, (12) radios and a T‐Arm style mount with pipe stiffeners.  The 86’ and 76’ elevations will include 
assumed  future  loading  similar  to  the  Verizon  antenna  array.   The  foundation  was  designed  based  on 
presumptive  soil  parameters  as  defined  in  Table  F‐1  of  the  ANSI/TIA‐222‐H  standard.   After  zoning 
approval has been granted, a full geotechnical investigation will be performed, and the final foundation 
design  will  be  reviewed  and  revised  as  needed  based  on  the  site‐specific  soil  strength  values.   Please 
reference  the  tower  and  foundation  design  calcs  and  drawings  prepared  by  Pinnacle  Consulting,  Inc., 
dated June 14, 2023 for more information regarding the analysis methods and capacities.

The proposed mount at the 96’ elevation consists of (1) MTSMC‐K10M‐12‐96 T‐arm mount with additional 
pipe stiffeners.  These are off‐the‐shelf antenna mounts that and the mounts were analyzed with the same 
design criteria listed above in accordance with the ANSI/TIA‐222‐H and TIA‐5053 standards.  All  of the 
steel
 pipe and HSS members were included in this analysis, as well as the mount connections back to the 
collar  mount  and  tower.   Please  reference  the  appurtenance  mount  analysis  prepared  by  Pinnacle 
Consulting, Inc., dated June 12, 2023 for more information regarding the analysis methods and capacities.

General Notes
1. The contractor and manufacturer shall verify all dimensions prior to fabrication
or construction and notify engineer of any noted discrepancy.
2. Typical details apply unless noted otherwise.
3. Drawings and calculations represent the finished conditions upon completion
of construction.  Contractor is responsible for means and methods including
rigging, shoring, temporary bracing, etc. as required to complete construction.
4. Drawings and calculations apply only to members specifically designed herein.
Structural Steel
1. Structural Steel shall conform to the following ASTM specifications:
Tapered Poles: A572, Grade 65
Baseplates (Up to 2" thick): A572, Grade 55
Baseplates (Over 2" thick): A572, Grade 50
Pipe: A53, Grade B (35 ksi)
Hollow Structural Sections: A500, Grade B (46 ksi)
Plate for Reinforced Ports: A572, Grade 65
All Other Shapes and Plates: A36
Bolts (High Strength): F3125, Grade A325
Anchor Bolts: A615, Grade 75 (as noted)
F1554, Grade 55 
2. High strength bolts shall be fully tensioned by turn-of-nut or
other AISC approved method.
3. Welding shall be performed by welders certified in the process and
position required in accordance with AWS D1.1 using 70 ksi electrodes,
unless noted otherwie.
4. All steel shall be hot dipped galvanized in accordance with ASTM A123.

he tower is a monopole.

The following design criteria apply:  
• Tower base elevation above sea level: 6301.20 ft.
• Basic wind speed of 115 mph.
• Risk Category II.
• Exposure Category C.
• Simplified Topographic Factor Procedure for wind speed-up calculations is used.
• Topographic Category: 1.
• Crest Height: 0.00 ft.
• Deflections calculated using a wind speed of 60 mph.
• A non-linear (P-delta) analysis was used.
• Pressures are calculated at each section.
• Stress ratio used in pole design is 1.
• Local bending stresses due to climbing loads, feed line supports, and appurtenance mounts are not considered.

Note that the values of moment capacity in the table above are not 
factored by a strength reduction factor (phi‐factor).
In ACI 318, the value of the strength reduction factor depends on whether 
the transverse reinforcing steel bars are tied hoops (0.65) or spirals (0.75).
The above values should be multiplied by the appropriate strength reduction 
factor to compute ultimate moment capacity according to ACI 318, 
or the value required by the design standard being followed.

bending stiffnesses computed for common resistance factor values used for

NAVAJO N  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC WORKS DEPARTMENT 
PLANNING & DEVELOPMENT 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: September 26th, 2023 
Case No:  SUP 23-008 
Action:   Special Use Permit  
Applicant/Owner: Pinnacle Consulting/Ralph E. and Julie A. Forney 
Project Name: VERIZON AZ11-172 STALLION WIRELESS COMMUNICATION 
FACILITY 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Recommendation: Staff recommends approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of 
this Staff Report. 
 
Location: The property is located in the Linden area; APN # 409-22-005C 
 
Size: One (1) parcel, totaling 5.32 acres 
 
Supervisor District: IV 
 
Zoning: A-General 
 
Property Owners: Ralph E. and Julie A. Forney 
 
Applicant: Pinnacle Consulting 
 
Representative: Graham Chapman 
 
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:  
Consideration of a request by Pinnacle Consulting for a Special Use Permit to allow for the construction 
of a 100’ mono-elm wireless communication tower and associated facilities in the Linden area. APN# 
409-22-005C. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
The majority of the land in the vicinity of this project is zoned  A-General and surrounded by a mix of 
residential and vacant lots.

<!-- page 615 -->
Board of Supervisors             2 | P a g e  
 
 
BACKGROUND/HISTORY: 
 
Currently, County records indicate an  existing residence on the property, as well as a commercial 
brewery on the west-side of the property. This proposal would expand the usage of the site to include a 
wireless communication tower and its associated facilities, which would be an appropriate usage within 
the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, if approved for a Special Use Permit.  
 
On the August 17th, 2023, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously 
recommended approval of the Special Use Permit for AZ11 -172 Stallion Wireless Communication 
Facility.  
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
The proposed project is a 100’ mono -elm wireless communication tower and its associated facilities. 
This tower will be located at least 100ft. away from all property lines on APN# 409-22-005C. The tower 
and all associated equipment will be located in a 40’ x 40’ area and surrounded by an 8ft. tall chain link 
fence, with 3-strand barbed wire around the top.  
 
ZONING DISTRICT: 
ARTICLE 3 – (A-GENERAL) – GENERAL ZONING DISTRICT 
 
Section 301- Purpose. 
 
The main purpose of this A-General Zone is to provide for all the unincorporated area of the County, except 
land within the boundary of a reservation and outside the authority of Navajo County not otherwise designated 
for some other specific zone, to be included in the "A-General Zone", by this Ordinance. No subdividing shall 
be conducted or approved in the "A -General Zone", without prior re -zoning of the land so intended. Uses 
permitted in the "A -General Zone", include farm and non -farm residential uses, f arms, recreational, 
institutional, commercial, and industrial uses as specifically listed in this Article. Other uses may be permitted 
as Special Uses under Article 20. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
  
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts 
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further 
prohibited by this Article. 
 
 34. Television, wireless towers, and radio transmitter towers and stations, subject  
 to approval by Federal Regulatory Agencies.  
 
Article 25 – General Provisions

<!-- page 616 -->
Board of Supervisors             3 | P a g e  
 
 
Section 2521 – Wireless Communication Facilities 
 
The purpose of this Section is to establish rules and regulations for the siting of wireless 
communications facilities. The goals of this Section are to provide for the development of 
wireless communication services County-wide by:  
 
1. Considering the public health, safety, and welfare. 
 
2. Considering impact on adjacent properties 
 
3. Encouraging configuration which minimizes additional visual impact through proper 
siting, design, landscape, and camouflage techniques.  
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
Comprehensive Plan 
 
Land Use Element 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, but 
not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping.  
 
• The applicant has indicated that they will setback their wireless communication tower a 
minimum of 100’ from all property lines, which is in line with the requirements of the 
Navajo County Zoning Ordinance for facilities of this nature. They have also indi cated 
a camouflaged, mono-elm design with the intent of helping the facility to blend in with 
the surrounding environment. Additionally, the applicant has indicated an 8ft. chain-link 
fence, lined with 3 -strand barbed wire, will be installed around the per imeter of the 
tower, which acts as a buffer between the proposed tower and existing residences in the 
subject area.  
 
Goal 9: Actively plan to accommodate growth in Navajo County in an appropriate manner. 
 
Policy 9-F: Encourage development/installation of technology improvements including, but not 
limited to, high speed Internet lines and other communications improvements in Navajo County. 
 
• The applicant is proposing the addition of this wireless communication tower and 
associated facilities to provide higher quality wireless service for residents, businesses, 
visitors, and emergency services in this region of Navajo County. 
 
Analysis: 
 
The proposed Special Use meets all relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance 
and the Navajo County  Comprehensive Plan as described above. This project, and its submittal, meet 
all requirements of the Section 2521 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, which regulates wireless

<!-- page 617 -->
Board of Supervisors             4 | P a g e  
 
communication facilities.  
 
Planning and Development Services Staff have  received one public comment in opposition of this 
proposal as of September 14th, 2023.  
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item was posted in the Wednesday, September 6th, edition of The Holbrook Tribune.  Postcards 
were mailed to neighbors within 300 feet of the property. The property was posted with public 
hearing notices, in accordance with the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance and Arizona Revised 
Statutes.  
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed project meets a ll applicable goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan, as 
noted within this report. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use is compatible with permissible uses within the A-General zoning district. 
 
4. The proposed use is  compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use.  
 
The proposed use has been determined to be compatible with current and likely future uses, per the 
Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.  
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
Given the nature of the property and the zoning of the ne ighborhood, there should be minimal 
adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity of the 
proposed use. 
 
PUBLIC WORKS AND OTHER COUNTY DEPARTMENTS’ COMMENTS: 
COUNTY ATTORNEY:  
Initial: B.C.  Date:   
      
ENGINEERING AND TRAFFIC: 
Initial: W.B. Date:  
   
DRAINAGE & FLOOD CONTROL: 
Initial: D.P.  Date:

<!-- page 618 -->
Board of Supervisors             5 | P a g e  
 
   
PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT: 
Initial: C.C.  Date:  
   
PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:  
 
The Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission and Navajo County Planning and Development 
Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) mono-elm wireless communication tower, not to 
exceed 100 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for operation of the tower on the 
subject property. 
 
2. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower and facilities 
prior to commencing construction. 
 
3. The applicant shall meet all State and Federal requirements concerning communication towers 
prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
4. The tower shall be constructed as a monopole, as indicated in the supplied plans.  The owner of 
the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
5. A fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director shall be constructed 
to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.  
 
6. A Geotechnical Report for the Access Easement shall be submitted and approved. 
 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any 
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this permit. 
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from the date of 
Board approval, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo County 
Planning and Development Services that the tower is no longer being utilized. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their expense, the 
removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users who may be 
interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such collocation upon commercially 
reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not technically feasible. 
 
12. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific use and 
to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final inspection by the Planning 
and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, signifying that all 
zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, 
the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the

<!-- page 619 -->
Board of Supervisors             6 | P a g e  
 
maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other 
provisions of this title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning and 
Development Staff on the fifth (5 th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and every five (5) 
years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in compliance with all conditions 
contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of compliance with these conditions, this Special 
Use Permit and its privileges may be revoked.

RESOLUTION ____-23 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS APPROVING 
A SPECIAL USE PERMIT FOR VERIZON AZ11-172 STALLION WIRELESS 
COMMUNICATION FACILITY 
 
WHEREAS, an application for a Special Use Permit was duly filed on June 1st, 2023 
by Graham Chapman to allow for the construction of a one-hundred foot (100’) mono-elm wireless 
communication tower and associated facility on a 5.32 acre parcel, in the Linden area; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application concerns the following real property in Navajo County: 
APN # 409-22-005C, Township 10 North, Range 2 1 East, Section 6 of the Gila and Salt River 
Base and Meridian; and 
 
WHEREAS, the application was considered by the Navajo County Planning & Zoning 
Commission at a duly noticed public hearing on August 17th, 2023; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Navajo County Board of Supervisors after considering the testimony 
and other evidence presented at the hearing, as well as the recommendations of Staff, found that 
the Special Use Permit is consistent with the public health, safety and general welfare and should 
be approved subject to the conditions set forth herein; and 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT R ESOLVED the Board of Supervisors  hereby 
approves the Special Use Permit, subject to the following conditions:  
 
1. This Special Use Permit shall permit 1 (one) mono-elm wireless communication 
tower, not to exceed 10 0 feet in height, and accessory structures necessary for 
operation of the tower on the subject property. 
 
2. The applicant shall obtain a Building Permit from Navajo County for such tower and 
facilities prior to commencing construction. 
 
3. The applicant shall meet all State and Federal requirements concerning 
communication towers prior to any building permits being issued. 
 
4. The tower shall be constructed as a mono-elm, as indicated in the supplied plans.  
The owner of the tower shall maintain the tower, antennae as necessary. 
 
5. A fence of material acceptable to the Planning and Development Services Director shall be 
constructed to enclose the base of the tower and necessary accessory structures.

<!-- page 621 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
6. A Geotechnical Report for the Access Easement shall be submitted and approved. 
 
7. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site 
plan.  Any expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to 
this permit. 
 
8. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period, from 
the date of Board approval, this permit shall become null and void. 
 
9. Should the operator decide to abandon the use of the tower, they shall notify Navajo 
County Planning and Development Services that the tower is no longer being 
utilized. 
 
10. If the tower is no longer being utilized, the property owner shall cause, at their 
expense, the removal of all components of this tower within a 90-day period. 
 
11. The owner shall provide reasonable cooperation to other public and private users 
who may be interested in collocation on this tower and shall consider such 
collocation upon commercially reasonable terms, unless such collocation is not 
technically feasible. 
 
12. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the 
specific use and to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final 
inspection by the Planning and Development Services Director (or designee) of any 
authorized structures, signifying that all zoning and site development requirements 
imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, the Special Use Permit shall 
thereafter be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the maintenance of special 
conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other provisions of this 
title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner. 
 
13. This Special Use Permit and the subject site shall be reviewed and inspected by Planning 
and Development Staff on the fifth (5th) anniversary of Board of Supervisors approval and 
every five (5) years thereafter to ensure the site is being utilized as approved and is in 
compliance with all conditions contained herein.  If the subject site is found to be out of 
compliance with these conditions, this Special Use Permit and its privileges may be 
revoked.
